Independent fleet views §

Selected-provider coast queries, per-view culling/cache and borrowed world reads.

What it does

This module provides per-view fleet culling and render list generation for secondary vessels and unpiloted objects. It uses orbit-annulus geometric bounds to cull ships outside the camera view before computing expensive Kepler solves, and caches solved positions while sub-pixel motion remains below a threshold, maintaining high frame rates across large fleets.

Provides and requires

  • Provides view.fleet@1.0.0 through the FLEET_VIEWS key: creates FlightFleetView instances for per-view ship list construction.
  • Fills the view.fleet slot in presentation profiles.
  • Requires no external services in its module manifest.

Settings

  • marginPixels (default 40, from 40 to 256, step 1): Viewport margin in CSS pixels matching the renderer margin, avoiding visual pop-in at screen edges.
  • stillPixels (default 0.25, from 0 to 1, step 0.05): Maximum CSS-pixel drift allowed before recomputing a cached coast position.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization captures marginPixels and stillPixels settings, contributes this guide, and registers FLEET_VIEWS.
  • create() produces an independent FlightFleetView for each viewport.
  • build(input) inspects borrowed world state, culls off-screen orbits, solves visible coast states, projects screen positions, and generates RenderShip records.
  • dispose clears placed ship caches, label strings, and world references.

Remove or replace

To replace fleet culling and rendering behaviors, provide view.fleet@1.0.0 in the view.fleet slot. Removing this module disables fleet presentation in multi-ship scenarios.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: fleet statistics structures and culling heuristics may evolve before API freeze.

Example

examples/modules/fleet-view demonstrates instantiating a fleet view, submitting frame parameters, and reading culling statistics. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/fleet-view/module.json /tmp/example-fleet-view.spacemod

Shared flight plans §

A shared service that stores maneuver plans as portable intent and checks them against the selected physics.

What it does

A flight plan is a list of maneuver nodes for one ship: at an absolute simulation time, add a velocity change made of prograde and radial parts, in m/s, around a named body. This module captures, validates and evaluates those plans. It stores intent only: no throttle, warp, arming or execution state. Evaluation uses the physics the game selected (motion, orbits, surfaces and rails) on a cloned context and never changes the live world.

Planners, transfer search and save files share one plan format, space-sim-flight-plans/1, so a plan made by one tool can be reviewed by another.

Provides and requires

  • Provides flight.plans@1.0.0 through the FLIGHT_PLANS key: a FlightPlanService.
  • Requires no services. Callers pass a WorldRead and ship. Finite-burn checks also need a ManeuverBurnFactory, which the host binds with withBurns.
  • Consumers in this checkout: spacelab.transfer-planner requires flight.plans@^1.0.0; spacelab.maneuver-editor declares it as optional; the Space Sim host uses it to validate plans stored in saves.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization provides one frozen, stateless service and this guide. The module owns no resources between calls.
  • capture snapshots the ship's current coast, the world identity and its motion model. read validates an untrusted archive: at most 64 plans of 16 nodes, node times increasing and within 100 Julian years of the plan's start. Unknown fields are dropped, and invalid input throws an Error that starts with "Flight plan:".
  • evaluate is a fast check for nodes around the starting body. A node on another body, a finite-burn node, or a coast that may cross an SOI or reach the surface first gets a reason asking for progressive evaluation.
  • evaluateAsync and predict are generators that do a bounded amount of work per step. They follow coasts through SOI changes and surface contact. They need world.predictionFeatures.futureSeeds, and endTime when a coast has to be traced rather than solved directly; older hosts get a reason instead. Close a generator you stop early with return(): this releases its forecast and cloned motion context.
  • Each evaluated node reports valid and a readable reason. A changed universe, system, physics fingerprint or motion model invalidates every node until the player reviews the plan; after the first failure, later nodes repeat its reason.

Remove or replace

Space Sim enables this module but does not list flight.plans as a required slot. Without it, the transfer planner cannot be enabled because it requires the service, the maneuver editor keeps working without shared plans, and a save that contains flight plans fails to load with a message asking for the module.

A replacement provides flight.plans@1.0.0, lists flight.plans in slots and is selected with "providers": { "flight.plans": "your.module-id" }. It must still read space-sim-flight-plans/1 archives, so that existing saves and other planners stay compatible.

Stability

Three versions apply here and change independently: the module version 0.1.0, the API identifier space-sim-game-api/2, and the archive format space-sim-flight-plans/1. The API and the manifest format, space-sim-module/2, are pre-freeze. A new archive format would need a migration so that older saves keep loading. Optional members of FlightPlanService, such as evaluateAsync and withBurns, may be missing on older providers; check for them before calling.

Example

examples/modules/flight-plans declares flight.plans as an optional service. It summarizes an archive when the service is present, reports the validation message when an archive is rejected, and still works when no provider is enabled. Build and check it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/flight-plans/module.json /tmp/plan-summary.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/plan-summary.spacemod

Related

Code modules: manifests, packaging and installation (in-game Manual: modules)

Progressive flight prediction: per-view previews of the current path

Progressive flight prediction §

Cancellable trajectory previews that a view can trace a little at a time without touching live flight.

What it does

A prediction (also called a ghost path) is the future trajectory drawn ahead of a ship. This module creates one preview per view and traces it progressively: each rendered frame does a small, bounded amount of work, so long paths appear over several frames instead of stalling one. Tracing runs on an isolated copy of the motion model, or through the world's own read-only predictor, and never advances the live simulation.

All values are body-relative SI: positions in metres, velocities in m/s and times in seconds of simulation time. Each sample may name its active body (bodyId) and the frame it is expressed in (frameId), a translating, nonrotating frame centred on the starting body. Transform both position and velocity through the system's ephemerides before comparing samples from different frames.

Provides and requires

  • Provides view.prediction@1.0.0 through the PREDICTIONS key: a PredictionFactory whose create(onComplete) returns a FlightPrediction.
  • Requires no services. Callers pass the SimulationContext, ship state, body and optional surface on every update, and optionally a WorldRead.
  • Consumers in this checkout: the flight host (Player 1's preview), spacelab.flight-split (guest seats) and spacelab.maneuver-editor, which both require view.prediction@^1.0.0.

Settings

  • maxMs (default 3, from 0.1 to 16, step 0.1): wall-clock milliseconds of tracing work allowed in one update of one preview. A consumer may lower FlightPrediction.maxMs for a single preview, for example a split-screen guest. Results do not change, only how quickly they appear.
  • traceSamplesPerSecond (default 1000, from 1 to 1000, step 1): samples of work earned per second of elapsed wall time for analytic coast previews.
  • numericalSamplesPerSecond (default 12000, from 120 to 24000, step 1): samples of work earned per second for previews that need step-by-step integration, such as terrain contact or shared-world rules.
  • Each update allows between 128 and 256 samples of work and stops early when maxMs is used up.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization provides the factory and registers it with context.own, so disposing the candidate disposes every preview it created.
  • create(onComplete) returns an independent preview with its own job, budget and two reusable sample buffers. onComplete runs once for each finished pass.
  • update starts a new pass when the ship, its status, the body, the surface, the motion model or a relevant command changes; otherwise it continues the current pass. While a replacement is tracing, path shows only the new partial path, never the stale one.
  • Pass world to trace through sphere-of-influence (SOI) changes with the host's world.predictShip; omit it for a body-only preview of the given state. In body-only mode, escape means the preview's distance limit was reached.
  • Read path and pathEnd. pathEnd is closed (a full orbit), impact, floating, escape, horizon (the predictor's time limit), open (a work limit, never a physical end), failed or grounded. A pass that reaches 32,768 samples ends as open.
  • invalidate cancels the pass and clears the path. Cancelling, finishing, a predictor error and dispose all close the job and its cloned context, even if one cleanup step fails. A disposed preview throws on update.

The path array is reused by a later pass. Copy samples you need to keep beyond the next update.

Remove or replace

Space Sim and Flight starter list view.prediction as a required slot, and split screen and the maneuver editor require it, so those games need a provider. A game that requires none of them can leave prediction out: flight, saves and multiplayer do not depend on it.

A replacement provides view.prediction@1.0.0 with its own PredictionFactory, lists view.prediction in slots, and is selected with "providers": { "view.prediction": "your.module-id" }. It must keep the same rules: no writes to the world, a bounded amount of work per update, and closing every job on invalidate and dispose.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze. FlightPrediction still exposes diagnostic fields such as passKey and lastPass that existing consumers read; treat them as diagnostics that may change before the API freeze.

Example

examples/modules/flight-prediction consumes view.prediction: it creates a preview, updates it until the pass finishes or a frame limit is reached, copies the result and disposes the preview. Build and check it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/flight-prediction/module.json /tmp/prediction-probe.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/prediction-probe.spacemod

Related

Code modules: manifests, packaging and installation (in-game Manual: modules)

game-api flight-view.ts: PredictionFactory and FlightPrediction

Determinism: why previews never change the live world (in-game Manual: determinism)

Flight split screen §

Independent local flight seats and split/dynamic panes over a borrowed session.

What it does

This module provides dynamic and fixed splitscreen flight views for up to four local players. When players fly together, they share a single adaptive camera pane; as vessels diverge past distance or screen thresholds, the viewport splits smoothly into independent player panes with dedicated cameras, trajectory predictions, fleet culling, and gamepad assignments.

Provides and requires

  • Provides view.flight-split@1.0.0 through the FLIGHT_SPLIT service key: creates FlightSplitScreen controllers managing guest seating and viewports.
  • Requires input.local-pilots@^1.0.0 through the LOCAL_PILOTS key: queries player color and profile preferences.
  • Requires input.flight-profiles@^1.0.0 through the FLIGHT_PROFILES key: manages controller mapping profiles.
  • Requires view.flight-camera@^1.0.0 through the FLIGHT_CAMERAS key: creates independent flight cameras for guest viewports.
  • Requires view.prediction@^1.0.0 through the PREDICTIONS key: allocates independent progressive trajectory previews.
  • Requires view.fleet@^1.0.0 through the FLEET_VIEWS key: builds per-pane fleet ship visibility lists.
  • Requires view.local@^1.0.0 through the LOCAL_VIEWS key: calculates responsive split pane rectangles.
  • Requires input.gamepad@^1.0.0 (optional) through the GAMEPAD_INPUT key: enables controller polling for guest joining.
  • Fills the view.flight-split slot in platform configurations.

Settings

  • joinButton (default 9, from 0 to 31, step 1): Standard gamepad button index that lets an unclaimed controller join local play. Default 9 is Start/Menu/Options; ownership rules still apply.
  • guestPredictionMs (default 1.5, from 0.1 to 10, step 0.1): Wall-time prediction budget for each guest view. Higher budgets refine previews sooner; they do not advance or alter the shared world.
  • wheelZoom (default 0.0015, from 0.0001 to 0.01, step 0.0001): Exponential zoom factor per mouse-wheel unit in guest panes. Each guest camera remains independent.
  • separateScreenRatio (default 0.55, from 0.1 to 1, step 0.01): Split a guest into its own pane when its screen distance exceeds this fraction of the viewport’s smaller dimension.
  • mergeScreenRatio (default 0.35, from 0.05 to 1, step 0.01): A separated guest must fall below this screen-distance fraction before merging. Must not exceed the separation threshold.
  • separateDistance (default 5000, from 1 to 1000000, step 1): Split a guest when its body-relative distance from Player 1 exceeds this distance, even if the shared camera is zoomed far out.
  • mergeDistance (default 3500, from 1 to 1000000, step 1): A guest must be closer than this distance before merging. Must not exceed the world separation threshold.
  • mergeDelayMs (default 600, from 0 to 10000, step 1): Keep a guest inside both merge thresholds for this long before returning to the shared view. Prevents rapid split/merge flicker.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization validates separation and merge threshold constraints, binds required input and camera services, contributes this guide, and registers FLIGHT_SPLIT.
  • create(viewport, host) mounts the splitscreen container and wires session callbacks.
  • pollPads() checks for join button presses on unassigned controllers and seats new players.
  • updateDynamic evaluates inter-ship separation and switches between single-view centroid tracking and multi-pane layouts.
  • dispose cleans up active guest cameras, prediction engines, fleet views, and gamepad instances.

Remove or replace

To customize multiplayer flight presentation, implement view.flight-split@1.0.0 in the view.flight-split slot. Removing this module disables local multi-seat flight, restricting games to single-player viewports.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: split-screen host interfaces and dynamic centroid algorithms may be refined prior to API freeze.

Example

examples/modules/flight-split demonstrates instantiating the flight split-screen manager and configuring local player seats. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/flight-split/module.json /tmp/example-flight-split.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-flight-split.spacemod

Related

HUD layout and visibility §

Optional viewer layout, movement and visibility with complete cleanup.

What it does

This module provides responsive HUD placement and interactive layout editing for flight instruments and UI controls. It enables dragging and keyboard repositioning of HUD elements, fits flexible instruments around obstacles, and provides a visibility controller for toggling individual instruments with persistent theme preferences.

Provides and requires

  • Provides ui.hud-layout@1.0.0 through the HUD_LAYOUT key: creates HudLayout responsive positioning controllers and HudVisibilityController managers.
  • Requires ui.windows@^1.0.0 through the WINDOWS key: uses window system icon assets for layout handles.
  • Fills the ui.hud-layout slot in platform profiles.

Settings

  • moveStepPx (default 8, from 1 to 128, step 1): Distance moved by one arrow-key press on a focused HUD grip in CSS pixels. Saved positions remain normalized to the viewport.
  • fastMoveStepPx (default 32, from 1 to 128, step 1): Distance moved by Shift plus an arrow key on a focused HUD grip in CSS pixels. Does not change pointer dragging or world coordinates.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization binds WINDOWS, loads layout stylesheets, captures moveStepPx and fastMoveStepPx, contributes this guide, and registers HUD_LAYOUT.
  • create(options) mounts layout styling and manages obstacle avoidance and drag bounds across viewport canvases.
  • visibility(options) instantiates visibility controllers managing toggle checkboxes and persisting display choices.
  • dispose cleanly detaches injected style elements, listeners, and interactive layout handles.

Remove or replace

To customize HUD layout behavior, provide an alternative implementation of ui.hud-layout@1.0.0 in the ui.hud-layout slot. Removing this module disables interactive HUD rearranging and visibility panels, leaving instruments at their default fixed coordinates.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: layout coordinate normalization formulas and visibility storage schemas may be adjusted prior to API freeze.

Example

examples/modules/hud-layout demonstrates configuring a HUD layout controller and obstacle avoidance. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/hud-layout/module.json /tmp/example-hud-layout.spacemod

Flight actions §

Namespaced flight actions and persistent keyboard bindings.

What it does

This module contributes the standard Space Sim flight action definitions (identified as spacelab.input-actions/<action>) to the module candidate and provides the input.flight-actions service. It defines whether each action is continuous (held controls like engine thrust, steering turns, throttle ramping and zoom) or discrete (press triggers like camera switching, time warp and pause). It also manages persistent keyboard bindings in browser localStorage, maps between namespaced and legacy action identifiers, and supplies localized action labels for configuration menus. It does not sample hardware devices, track player seats or advance simulation worlds.

Provides and requires

  • Provides input.flight-actions@1.0.0 through the FLIGHT_ACTIONS key: action definitions, human-readable labels, and load/save/reset keybinding operations.
  • The module itself requires no services.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization contributes FLIGHT_ACTION_DEFINITIONS to the candidate registry and registers the FLIGHT_ACTIONS service object. The module allocates no running background tasks.
  • load reads active bindings from localStorage key space-sim:keybindings:v1, migrating former default throttle bindings while preserving customized keys and unknown action identifiers.
  • save serializes current bindings back to localStorage, translating namespaced action identifiers back into legacy keys.
  • reset clears stored keybindings and restores factory default key assignments.
  • Disposal requires no resource cleanup because the provider holds no DOM nodes, hardware listeners or timers.

Remove or replace

Games without flight simulation or using entirely custom control schemes can omit this module. Games providing flight control menus require an input.flight-actions provider. To replace it, create a platform-tier module providing input.flight-actions@1.0.0 and register it under "providers": { "input.flight-actions": "your.module-id" }.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: action contracts and keybinding representations may change before API freeze. Stored keyboard preferences use key space-sim:keybindings:v1.

Example

examples/modules/input-actions is a consumer module that uses input.flight-actions@1.0.0 to inspect flight action definitions, query friendly action labels and summarize active keyboard assignments. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/input-actions/module.json /tmp/input-actions-example.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/input-actions-example.spacemod

Gamepad input §

Samples generic namespaced actions from standard gamepad buttons and axes; retains editable flight mappings and diagnostics.

What it does

This module samples standard W3C Gamepad API controllers into namespaced actions for active local player seats (pilots 0–3). It provides the input.gamepad service, which creates generic InputSource instances for custom action bindings and provides createFlight to instantiate a FlightGamepad manager for flight HUDs and controls. It also formats physical button indices into platform-appropriate labels (such as A, Cross, or B) across Xbox, PlayStation, Steam Deck, Nintendo and generic controller families. It does not step physics worlds or manage network session identity.

Provides and requires

  • Provides input.gamepad@1.0.0 through the GAMEPAD_INPUT key: generic gamepad input source factory, flight gamepad manager, and family-aware button label formatting.
  • Requires input.flight-profiles@^1.0.0 (optional) for flight gamepad mappings; generic gamepad sources work without it.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization registers the GAMEPAD_INPUT service and tracks created sources and managers for automatic disposal. The module allocates no background polling loops until requested.
  • create returns an InputSource with validated deadzone in [0, 0.5], pilot index in [0, 3], and at most 256 axis or button bindings.
  • sample reads navigator.getGamepads synchronously. Disconnecting or reassigning a pad clears held values and inhibits input until axes and buttons return within 0.25 of neutral. Press actions fire when crossing 0.25; continuous actions emit deadzone-filtered values.
  • createFlight requires an input.flight-profiles provider and throws if none is bound.
  • Provider disposal releases all active sources and flight managers, throwing an AggregateError if cleanup fails.

Remove or replace

Games without controller support can omit this module. The flight controls menu adapts gracefully when input.gamepad is absent: keyboard-controlled seats and profile customization remain functional while live hardware diagnostics are disabled. To replace it, build a platform-tier module providing input.gamepad@1.0.0.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: controller contracts and diagnostic telemetry may change before API freeze. Flight profiles migrate from storage key space-sim:gamepad:v1.

Example

examples/modules/input-gamepad is a consumer module that uses input.gamepad@1.0.0 to configure and sample a custom gamepad input source with custom deadzone and action bindings. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/input-gamepad/module.json /tmp/input-gamepad-example.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/input-gamepad-example.spacemod

Keyboard input §

Samples generic namespaced actions from a keyboard; preserves the existing flight keyboard/hold-button adapter.

What it does

This module translates DOM keyboard events into namespaced actions for active local player seats (pilots 0–3). It provides the input.keyboard service through the KEYBOARD_INPUT key. It attaches window and document event listeners, automatically ignores keystrokes originating inside form controls or links, and evaluates modifier chords (Ctrl, Meta, Shift) before bare keys. It also clears active keys when the browser window loses focus or hides, and provides the legacy bindFlight adapter for existing flight games alongside the reserved browser keys list.

Provides and requires

  • Provides input.keyboard@1.0.0 through the KEYBOARD_INPUT key: keyboard input source factory, legacy flight keyboard adapter, and reserved keys set.
  • The module itself requires no services.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization provides KEYBOARD_INPUT and registers a cleanup lease that tracks created sources and flight adapters.
  • create attaches keydown, keyup, blur, and pagehide listeners to window, plus visibilitychange on document.
  • sample checks frame eligibility, evaluates active keys for the assigned pilot, and writes triggered actions to the sink. Press actions fire on initial press and clear after the frame; held actions persist until key release.
  • Window blur, document hiding, pilot seat changes, or capture returning false immediately clears all held keys.
  • dispose removes all attached window and document listeners. Provider disposal disposes all tracked sources, throwing an AggregateError if any cleanup throws.

Remove or replace

Included in planet-viewer and survey-fixture game targets. Games requiring virtual on-screen keyboards or specialized accessibility inputs can replace this module with another provider of input.keyboard@1.0.0 in their game profile.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: options and legacy chord bindings may be revised before API freeze.

Example

examples/modules/input-keyboard is a consumer module that uses input.keyboard@1.0.0 to create an InputSource for custom action shortcuts and sample them synchronously. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/input-keyboard/module.json /tmp/input-keyboard-example.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/input-keyboard-example.spacemod

Flight controller profiles §

Editable flight mappings and legacy controller preference migration.

What it does

This module provides the input.flight-profiles service through the FLIGHT_PROFILES key. It owns standard controller mappings for flight actions across standard controller layouts (turn, throttle, and zoom axes, plus button bindings for flight and view actions). It validates, sanitizes, and normalizes per-player controller profiles, ensuring axis indices and button bindings stay within valid bounds, and provides migration from legacy storage key space-sim:gamepad:v1. It does not interact with hardware devices, attach event listeners, or step physics worlds.

Provides and requires

  • Provides input.flight-profiles@1.0.0 through the FLIGHT_PROFILES key: default controller profile, profile normalizer, and legacy migration codec.
  • The module itself requires no services.

Settings

  • defaultDeadzone (default 0.15, from 0 to 0.5, step 0.01): radial stick deadzone fraction used when an imported or stored profile omits an explicit deadzone setting.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization reads context.settings.defaultDeadzone, constructs the profile codec, and registers the FLIGHT_PROFILES service object. The module holds no running tasks.
  • normalize validates and clamps deadzones into [0, 0.5], keeps at most 4 valid button indices (0–31) per action, drops unrecognized actions, and resolves former default binding collisions.
  • legacy inspects localStorage key space-sim:gamepad:v1 to migrate older version 1 player controller settings into a normalized profile.
  • Disposal requires no resource cleanup because the codec owns no event listeners, DOM elements or hardware timers.

Remove or replace

Space Sim and Flight starter list input.flight-profiles as a required slot. To replace it, build a platform-tier module providing input.flight-profiles@1.0.0 with custom profile defaults or migration rules, and select it in the game profile with "providers": { "input.flight-profiles": "your.module-id" }.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: profile structures and codec interfaces may evolve before API freeze. Stored controller preferences migrate from key space-sim:gamepad:v1.

Example

Touch input §

Samples caller-supplied pointer controls and namespaced actions; retains the editable flight overlay and its styles.

What it does

This module translates pointer gestures on caller-supplied HTML elements into namespaced action values for active local player seats (pilots 0–3). It provides the input.touch service through the TOUCH_INPUT key. It tracks hold buttons, horizontal axes (axis-x), and vertical axes (axis-y) using Pointer Events and pointer capture, clamping axis values to [-1, 1]. It also provides createFlight to instantiate the legacy TouchControlsManager flight overlay, with visibility preferences saved under storage key space-sim:touch-controls:v1. It does not advance simulation time or step physics worlds.

Provides and requires

  • Provides input.touch@1.0.0 through the TOUCH_INPUT key: pointer gesture input source factory and flight touch overlay manager.
  • The module itself requires no services.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization registers the TOUCH_INPUT service and tracks all active sources and managers for automatic disposal. The module creates no DOM elements until requested.
  • create attaches pointerdown, pointermove, pointerup, pointercancel, and lostpointercapture listeners across up to 256 caller-supplied elements.
  • sample evaluates active gestures for the assigned pilot, writing active values in [-1, 1] into the action sink. Continuous gestures emit updated fractions; press gestures pulse once on initial contact.
  • Window blur, pagehide, document hiding, or pilot seat changes immediately release active pointer captures and reset gesture values to zero.
  • dispose removes all element and window event listeners. Provider disposal releases all created sources and overlay managers, throwing an AggregateError if cleanup fails.

Remove or replace

Games without touch devices or pointer-based on-screen controls can omit this module. To replace it, build a platform-tier module providing input.touch@1.0.0.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: pointer gesture definitions and overlay manager contracts may change before API freeze. Flight touch settings persist under storage key space-sim:touch-controls:v1.

Example

Server pack review §

Exact package review and explicit consent with Cancel focused by default.

What it does

This module renders an accessible modal dialog prompting the user to review and approve game packages required by a remote multiplayer server. It details the server endpoint, total download size, required module versions, licenses, architectural tiers, and exact SHA-256 digests. If an offer contains executable code, it presents an explicit security warning and places default keyboard focus on the Cancel button to prevent accidental approval.

Provides and requires

  • Provides ui.join-review@1.0.0 through the JOIN_REVIEW key: a JoinReviewService instance.
  • Requires no services.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization instantiates and provides the JoinReviewService, registered for owned disposal.
  • review(offer, address, signal) creates and mounts a standard HTML dialog element, styling it according to active UI theme variables.
  • The dialog autofocuses the Cancel action; pressing Escape or clicking Cancel rejects the offer, resolving the promise to false.
  • Selecting 'Install and join' approves the offer, resolving to true.
  • Aborting the signal cancels the dialog and removes it from the document body.
  • Disposing the service cancels any pending review modal and cleans up active listeners.

Remove or replace

This module is a platform-tier presentation provider designed for browser environments. Headless dedicated servers do not need it. In custom client distributions or automated test rigs, it can be replaced by a module that automatically accepts or filters packages. To replace it, author a module providing ui.join-review@1.0.0 implementing JoinReviewService.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze. Dialog presentation and styling conform to current Spacelab UI theme guidelines.

Example

examples/modules/join-review demonstrates creating a custom automated review service that verifies required package tiers without displaying DOM modals. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/join-review/module.json /tmp/join-review-example.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/join-review-example.spacemod

Local pilot registry §

Adapts one to four active local seats to borrowed session identities and device preferences.

What it does

This module adapts 1 to 4 active local player seats (pilots 0–3) to host session identities and device assignments. It provides the input.local-pilots service through the LOCAL_PILOTS key. It offers create to wrap a LocalPilotHost into a PilotRegistry managing device assignments ('auto', 'keyboard', 'none', or 'pad:N') and seat release callbacks. When a compatible input.flight-profiles provider is present, its preferences method returns a LocalPlayerPreferences codec that normalizes, loads, and saves per-player names, device choices, and navball screen anchors. It does not advance simulation worlds or grant network session authority.

Provides and requires

  • Provides input.local-pilots@1.0.0 through the LOCAL_PILOTS key: local pilot registry factory and local player preferences codec.
  • Requires input.flight-profiles@^1.0.0 (optional) for flight player preference defaults; generic local seat registries work without it.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization registers LOCAL_PILOTS and creates an internal tracking set to dispose all active registries when the module is torn down.
  • create wraps a caller-provided LocalPilotHost into a LocalPilotRegistry, ensuring active seat counts stay within [1, 4].
  • assign validates device identifiers and releases held inputs across the host before assigning the device to prevent stuck input across hot-swapped controllers.
  • preferences constructs a LocalPlayerPreferences codec backed by input.flight-profiles, throwing an error if that optional provider is absent.
  • dispose calls host.release and marks the registry as disposed. Provider disposal tears down all active registries, throwing an AggregateError if cleanup fails.

Remove or replace

Space Sim and Flight starter specify input.local-pilots as a required slot. To replace it, build a platform-tier module providing input.local-pilots@1.0.0 with custom seat rules (such as fixed arcade seat bindings or single-pilot limits) and select it with "providers": { "input.local-pilots": "your.module-id" }.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: pilot registry contracts and preference structures may change before API freeze.

Example

examples/modules/local-pilots is a consumer module that implements a headless LocalPilotHost to manage seat device assignments through LOCAL_PILOTS. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/local-pilots/module.json /tmp/local-pilots-example.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/local-pilots-example.spacemod

Independent local views §

Allocates independent camera/surface lifetimes and the existing split/dynamic pane layouts.

What it does

This module allocates independent local viewport lifetimes, camera instances, and rendering surfaces for up to four local players. It computes split and dynamic pane rectangles across side-by-side, stacked, and automatic layouts, maintaining independent zoom, orientation, and focus bindings across viewports without advancing simulation state.

Provides and requires

  • Provides view.local@1.0.0 through the LOCAL_VIEWS key: creates LocalViews multi-pane managers and calculates pane layout partitions.
  • Requires view.camera@^1.0.0 through the VIEW_CAMERAS key: creates independent LocalViewCamera instances for each bound viewport.
  • Fills the view.local slot in platform profiles.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization binds VIEW_CAMERAS, contributes this guide, and registers the LOCAL_VIEWS provider.
  • create(options) validates 1 to 4 distinct seat and viewport bindings, instantiates independent cameras and surfaces, and returns a LocalViews controller.
  • reflow(width, height, separatedGuests) recalculates pane rectangles, toggles pane visibility, and notifies each surface of its resized bounds.
  • render(elapsed, transform) steps each visible camera frame and renders projected scenes across active surfaces.
  • dispose cleanly releases all child surface adapters and camera instances.

Remove or replace

This module can be replaced by any provider supplying view.local@1.0.0 in the view.local slot. Removing it disables local multi-view partitioning and splitscreen pane allocations.

Stability

Module logging §

Bounded module-scoped diagnostics with severity filtering and no simulation state.

What it does

Provides bounded in-memory diagnostics for browser, offline and Node hosts. Each entry records module ID and exact version, severity, event name, message, optional scalar fields and monotonic diagnostic wall milliseconds (timeMs), which is unrelated to simulation time and never belongs in authoritative data. Entries are immutable snapshots in a ring buffer; filtered, invalid, excess and overwritten entries are dropped. Never log every frame, serialize live worlds or use logs for game logic.

Provides and requires

  • Provides diagnostics.logs@1.0.0 through the MODULE_LOGS service key: creates scoped loggers and queries entries.
  • Fills the diagnostics.logs slot in platform profiles.
  • Requires nothing. Consumers declare diagnostics.logs@^1.0.0 as an optional requirement, capture it with tryUse during initialization and own their scoped logger. Tier rules keep authoritative and presentation modules from depending on a platform provider.

Settings

  • minimumLevel (default 1, from 0 to 3, step 1): lowest accepted severity, 0 debug, 1 info, 2 warn, 3 error.
  • historyEntries (default 512, from 1 to 10000, step 1): ring size in entries; older entries are overwritten.
  • maxPerSecond (default 100, from 1 to 10000, step 1): accepted entries per logger per wall-clock second; the excess is dropped.
  • messageLength (default 2048, from 64 to 8192, step 1): characters kept per message; longer text is truncated.
  • consoleOutput (default 0, from 0 to 1, step 1): 1 also mirrors each stored entry to the console.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization reads settings, creates the provider, registers MODULE_LOGS, contributes this guide and logs logging.ready for itself.
  • logger(module) validates the module ID and exact version and returns a logger. write checks severity and rate first, evaluates a lazy message only then, validates fields (at most 32, keys up to 64 characters, strings up to 1024) and stores a frozen entry. Invalid input, a failing sink or clock never affects the caller.
  • entries(query) returns oldest-first immutable snapshots filtered by module, minimum level, sequence cursor and limit. There is no live subscription; do not poll it in a frame loop. clear() empties the ring.
  • The provider owns history; the consumer owns its logger and disposes it on removal. Disposing the provider makes stale loggers inert and entries() return an empty list.

Remove or replace

Provide another diagnostics.logs@1.0.0 implementation in the diagnostics.logs slot to change retention or output, or change the settings above. Removing the module leaves consumers working without diagnostics, because they treat the requirement as optional. A broken or incompatible selected provider still fails service resolution.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: details may change before the final freeze.

Example

examples/modules/logging wraps a scoped logger and reads back entries through the public diagnostics.logs service. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/logging/module.json /tmp/example-logging.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-logging.spacemod

Related

Code modules: manifests, packaging and installation (in-game Manual: modules)

API reference: Module logging

game-api: logging.ts

Finite maneuver burns §

Validated fixed-tick intent with compensated impulse accounting; the host owns commands and stepping.

What it does

This module provides finite maneuver burn intent calculation in the body's inertial reference frame. It computes burn start epochs centered on the scheduled impulse time, guides ship attitude alignment prior to ignition, requests fractional throttle on boundary ticks, and accumulates delivered velocity changes with compensated summation to prevent drift during prolonged thrust.

Provides and requires

  • Provides flight.maneuver-burn@1.0.0 through the MANEUVER_BURNS service key: creates ManeuverBurnControl controllers.
  • Fills the flight.maneuver-burn slot in platform profiles.
  • Requires core.clock@^1.0.0: operates over a compatible fixed 0.01-second physics clock.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization captures the core.clock service and registers the MANEUVER_BURNS provider.
  • create(time, delta, vehicle) calculates the burn start epoch half the total burn duration before the target impulse time.
  • next(state) is called once before each fixed physics tick at 1x time warp, commanding alignment turns, waiting until start, or commanding engine thrust.
  • The controller transitions through aligning, waiting, burning, and complete phases, releasing pending controls upon completion.
  • dispose marks pending intent inert without mutating authoritative world state.

Remove or replace

To customize finite burn integration or attitude steering heuristics, contribute an alternative flight.maneuver-burn@1.0.0 provider to the flight.maneuver-burn slot. Removing this module leaves flight planning without finite burn execution capability.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: steering error thresholds and compensated impulse accounting may evolve prior to final freeze.

Example

examples/modules/maneuver-burn demonstrates instantiating a finite burn controller, stepping through alignment phases, and verifying delivered delta-v. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/maneuver-burn/module.json /tmp/example-maneuver-burn.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-maneuver-burn.spacemod

Independent maneuver editors §

Read-only candidate previews and validated host commands over shared floating windows.

What it does

This module provides floating window and interactive map overlay editors for creating, adjusting, validating, and executing orbital maneuver plans. It computes candidate trajectory forecasts over isolated prediction jobs, manages prograde and radial impulse adjustments, coordinates automatic attitude alignment, and communicates validated execution commands to the simulation host.

Provides and requires

  • Provides view.maneuver-planner@1.0.0 through the MANEUVER_PLANNERS service key: creates ManeuverPlannerControl instances.
  • Fills the view.maneuver-planner slot in platform profiles.
  • Requires core.motion@^1.0.0: uses simulation context motion services for orbital state evaluation.
  • Requires core.clock@^1.0.0: coordinates execution with fixed simulation clock steps.
  • Requires ui.windows@^1.0.0: mounts floating editor windows within the user interface.
  • Requires view.prediction@^1.0.0: generates trajectory coast and encounter predictions.
  • Requires flight.maneuver-burn@^1.0.0: executes finite burn attitude alignment and throttle commands.
  • Requires view.maneuver-display@^1.0.0: provides maneuver overlay presentation styling.
  • Requires flight.plans@^1.0.0 (optional): adds multi-node plan chains, serialization, and save integration.
  • Requires flight.transfers@^1.0.0 (optional): embeds automated orbital transfer window searching.
  • Requires flight.alarms@^1.0.0 (optional): connects burn reminders and automated warp release.

Settings

  • previewMaxMs (default 1, from 0.1 to 8, step 0.1): Per-update wall-time budget in milliseconds for the independent planned-burn preview.
  • uiRefreshMs (default 100, from 16 to 1000, step 1): Minimum wall time in milliseconds between editor status and summary refreshes.
  • alignmentWarp (default 4, from 1 to 4, step 1): Automatic alignment warp multiplier during explicitly authorized attitude alignment.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization resolves required and optional services, injects stylesheet assets, and registers MANEUVER_PLANNERS.
  • create(options) mounts an independent floating window, attaches optional map drag handlers, and initializes preview workers.
  • step(manual) advances active burn execution before each simulation tick, applying host-authorized controls.
  • dispose cancels pending actions, releases held host controls, detaches DOM event listeners, and closes the editor window.

Remove or replace

To replace the maneuver planner UI or candidate node workflow, provide view.maneuver-planner@1.0.0 in the view.maneuver-planner slot. Removing this module removes maneuver editing windows and node manipulation while retaining background prediction and telemetry.

Stability

Manual reader and guide catalog §

Optional Players/Creators navigation over shared documents with explicit live-content adapters.

What it does

Provides the Manual reader and a removable catalog of guide pages. Readers navigate Players and Creators books over structured documents, search contributed body text and can mount trusted live content through host adapters. It has no simulation, pilot, input or tutorial dependency, and accepts no raw imported HTML.

Provides and requires

  • Provides ui.manual@1.0.0 through the MANUAL_READER service key: guide registration, guides(audience) and reader creation.
  • Fills the ui.manual slot in platform profiles.
  • Requires ui.window-content@^1.0.0 through the WINDOW_CONTENT service key: renders structured documents.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization captures WINDOW_CONTENT, registers MANUAL_READER with the shared styles and contributes this guide.
  • register(pages, options) adds pages with a stable ID, title, summary and audience and returns a lease. Disposing it refreshes readers and falls back to an available page. register(pages, {replace: true}) shadows matching IDs: releasing the candidate restores earlier guides, releasing the earlier lease keeps the new ones. Duplicate IDs within one registration always fail.
  • guides(audience) lists contributed pages without mounting a reader, so recovery and library UIs can use them.
  • create(options) builds a reader from a book, root, optional preference storage and a unique idPrefix. A host-supplied mount callback and revision string enable trusted live content; refresh updates the body only when the page or revision changes. Storage failures leave navigation usable for the session.
  • Disposing a reader aborts its handlers and restores borrowed root children and classes. Disposing the provider disposes readers and styles and rejects new creation. Use separate idPrefix and storageKey values for independent readers.

Remove or replace

Provide another ui.manual@1.0.0 implementation in the ui.manual slot to change how guides are presented. Removing the module removes the reader and guide catalog; other modules keep working, but contributed guides are no longer shown in game. The trusted default Space Sim book lives in games/space-sim/manual.ts and its control adapter composes it separately. Other games supply their own books and register structured guides through this same service; no flight chapters are added by the reader.

Stability

Module version 0.1.2 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: details may change before the final freeze.

Example

examples/modules/manual publishes and withdraws a Creators guide page through the public ui.manual service. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/manual/module.json /tmp/example-manual.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-manual.spacemod

ModMenu library view §

Metadata browsing and explicit cancellable host commands without archive or world ownership.

What it does

Provides a reusable library and module-management view for browser and offline hosts. Each view takes explicit DOM roles and a host port. The host owns verified packages, persistence, code approval, exact profiles, world publication and download encoding; the view shows display metadata only and never receives archive blobs or live worlds. Importing inert bytes, editing a draft and enabling a game stay separate explicit actions.

Provides and requires

  • Provides ui.mod-menu@1.0.0 through the MOD_MENU service key: creates library, module and profile views.
  • Fills the ui.mod-menu slot in platform profiles.
  • Requires nothing; initialization needs no other capability. Each view needs a host port: ModMenuLibraryPort (library), ModMenuModulePort (module drafts) or ModMenuProfilePort (legacy profile).
  • Also exports mountBundledCatalogue(root, entries): an inert searchable inventory of bundled source packages that never installs anything.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization registers MOD_MENU with the shared styles and contributes this guide.
  • library(elements, port) browses entries (opaque ID, purpose, collection, favorite, built-in status, code/data counts). Hosts may pin an entry ahead of favorites; search and favorite filters still apply.
  • modules(root, port, options) edits exact v2 release, capability, provider and setting drafts. The host's compatible() supplies suggestions; the view contains no second dependency resolver. profile(elements, port, initial) keeps the legacy v1 profile editor, whose draft() does not manufacture a v2 lock.
  • Import, save, export, play, organize and remove are async host commands with an AbortSignal, one at a time. The host must check cancellation before changing the active world, validate and stage a compatible candidate before publishing, and keep active artifacts protected from removal.
  • Rebuilding cards aborts their handlers. Disposing a view aborts its pending command, restores borrowed list, status and disabled state and ignores late results. The provider owns views and styles and rejects creation after disposal.

Remove or replace

Provide another ui.mod-menu@1.0.0 implementation in the ui.mod-menu slot for a different library UI. Removing the module removes the library view, so keep a minimal import and dependency recovery path in the host. Hierarchical module folders, a collection archive format, workers, a marketplace and version upgrades are not implemented.

Stability

Module version 0.2.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: details may change before the final freeze.

Example

examples/modules/mod-menu mounts the exact-release module picker through the public ui.mod-menu service with a host port. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/mod-menu/module.json /tmp/example-mod-menu.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-mod-menu.spacemod

Per-view navball §

Optional attitude instrument using the shared HUD lifecycle.

What it does

This module provides a flight attitude and velocity vector navball instrument rendered as vector overlay primitives. It displays ship pitch, heading, roll, prograde, retrograde, normal, anti-normal, radial, anti-radial, and maneuver target vectors across orbital and rotating surface reference frames without modifying flight mechanics.

Provides and requires

  • Fills the hud.navball slot in presentation profiles.
  • Requires no external services in its module manifest.

Settings

  • frame (default 0, from 0 to 2, step 1): Velocity reference frame mode: 0 automatically selects surface or orbital frame, 1 forces orbital frame, and 2 forces rotating surface frame.
  • orientation (default 0, from 0 to 1, step 1): Attitude dial orientation: 0 keeps local horizon upright, 1 rotates dial with camera orientation.
  • minimumSpeed (default 0.05, from 0 to 10, step 0.01): Direction marker noise floor in metres per second. Hides prograde/retrograde markers below this speed without modifying physics.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization captures frame, orientation, and minimumSpeed settings, contributes this guide, and provides NAVBALL_HUD.
  • The instrument creates per-view instances using FLIGHT_HUD_STATE adapters supplied by the HUD context.
  • draw(into) samples ship attitude, velocity vectors, and maneuver nodes, pushing vector overlay circles, crosshairs, and markers into the overlay buffer.
  • dispose clears cached frame references and releases gesture bindings.

Remove or replace

Node WebSocket server transport §

Editable WebSocket protocol over a borrowed HTTP upgrade and native byte codecs.

What it does

This module provides server-side WebSocket protocol termination for Node.js dedicated servers. It accepts incoming HTTP upgrade requests over borrowed byte streams, handles Sec-WebSocket-Key cryptographic handshakes, manages RFC 6455 data framing and unmasking, maintains keep-alive ping/pong heartbeats, and negotiates permessage-deflate compression using native engine codecs. The HTTP port listener remains host-owned.

Provides and requires

  • Provides net.node-ws@1.0.0 through the NODE_WEBSOCKET_TRANSPORT key: a NodeWebSocketProvider instance.
  • Requires core.websocket-codecs@^1.0.0 through the NODE_WEBSOCKET_CODECS key: engine-supplied native SHA-1 and DEFLATE codecs.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization resolves the required WebSocketCodecs service and provides the NodeWebSocketProvider, registering it for owned disposal.
  • accept(request, stream, head, options) inspects upgrade headers, completes the HTTP switching-protocols handshake, and returns a ServerTextConnection, or undefined if the request is invalid.
  • ServerTextConnection extends TextConnection with a closed promise that resolves once TCP termination and handshake grace periods conclude.
  • Disposing the provider cleanly closes all active server connections with reason 'Module disposed'.

Remove or replace

This module is restricted to the Node environment. Browser clients and offline single-player modes do not load or require it. Dedicated server hosts running multiplayer sessions need a provider for net.node-ws. To replace it, author a network-tier module that provides net.node-ws@1.0.0 implementing NodeWebSocketProvider.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: server transport interfaces may be revised prior to API stabilization.

Example

examples/modules/net-node-ws demonstrates upgrading HTTP requests into ServerTextConnection instances with configured message limits. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/net-node-ws/module.json /tmp/node-ws-example.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/node-ws-example.spacemod

Scoped tab transport §

Same-origin text links and discovery scoped by game and session protocol.

What it does

This module enables multiplayer sessions across multiple browser tabs within the same origin without sending network packets outside the machine. It uses the browser BroadcastChannel API for message passing and Web Locks for host leader election and connection coordination. Each network is strictly scoped by namespace and protocol ID to avoid cross-game interference.

Provides and requires

  • Provides net.tab@1.0.0 through the TAB_TRANSPORT key: a TabNetworkFactory creating scoped TabNetwork instances.
  • Requires no services.

Settings

  • beatMs (default 2000, from 1 to 300000, step 1): Heartbeat spacing for same-browser peers when Web Locks are unavailable in milliseconds. No network traffic leaves this browser.
  • idleTimeoutMs (default 30000, from 1 to 300000, step 1): Wait this long without peer liveness before closing a same-browser connection in milliseconds. Keep it comfortably longer than the heartbeat interval.
  • connectTimeoutMs (default 5000, from 1 to 300000, step 1): Maximum wait for a hosting tab to answer a new connection request in milliseconds. Independent of established-peer liveness.
  • maxTextLength (default 67108864, from 1 to 67108864, step 1): Maximum UTF-16 characters in an application message and queued backlog. Lowering it can reject large game snapshots or join packages.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization verifies that idleTimeoutMs exceeds beatMs and provides the TabNetworkFactory.
  • create(options) builds a TabNetwork managing channel multiplexing for a specific game scope and protocol ID.
  • network.host(describe, accept, signal) registers a tab session, broadcasts advertisements, and receives connections.
  • network.watch(change, signal) listens for active tab hosts, providing reactive updates to discovery menus.
  • network.connect(address, signal) opens a TextConnection to a tab:// URL using an AbortSignal.
  • Disposing the network closes all channels, releases locks, and disconnects all open connections.

Remove or replace

Removing this module disables local same-browser multi-tab sessions. Single-player modes, split-screen play, and remote WebSocket multiplayer are unaffected. To replace it, author a module providing net.tab@1.0.0 with a compatible TabNetworkFactory.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze. Tab connection URLs use the tab:// URI scheme with a 32-character hexadecimal host identifier.

Example

examples/modules/net-tab demonstrates creating a scoped tab network, publishing a session advert, and discovering peers. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/net-tab/module.json /tmp/tab-example.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/tab-example.spacemod

Related

Manual WebRTC transport §

Manual invite/reply signaling over reliable ordered text, independent of session state.

What it does

This module enables serverless peer-to-peer multiplayer by exchanging manual copy-paste invite and reply text codes between players. It encapsulates WebRTC RTCPeerConnection management, gathers ICE candidates with optional STUN server assistance, compresses SDP negotiation data into compact base64url strings, and establishes an ordered, reliable RTCDataChannel exposed as a standard TextConnection.

Provides and requires

  • Provides net.webrtc-signaling@1.0.0 through the WEBRTC_SIGNALING key: a ManualSignalingProvider instance.
  • Requires no services.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization provides the ManualSignalingProvider and registers it for owned disposal.
  • The host calls create(stun, signal), returning a ManualInvite with a compact offer code and an accept(reply) function.
  • The joining guest calls answer(offer, signal), returning a ManualReply with an answer code and a connection Promise.
  • The host feeds the guest's reply code into accept(reply), establishing the bidirectional TextConnection.
  • Aborting the signal cancels pending candidate gathering and closes any in-flight peer connection.
  • Provider disposal closes all active peer connections and terminates gathering.

Remove or replace

Removing this module disables manual invite-code peer-to-peer multiplayer. Dedicated server connections via WebSocket and same-origin tab sessions remain fully functional. To replace it, author a network-tier module that provides net.webrtc-signaling@1.0.0 implementing ManualSignalingProvider.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze. Invitation and reply codes use compact format prefixes SSO1 (Space Sim Offer v1) and SSA1 (Space Sim Answer v1) with compressed SDP payloads.

Example

examples/modules/net-webrtc demonstrates generating invitation codes, validating format strings, and checking connection diagnostics. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/net-webrtc/module.json /tmp/webrtc-example.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/webrtc-example.spacemod

WebSocket transport §

An ordered reliable text transport without flight or session implementation imports.

What it does

This module provides client-side WebSocket communication for connecting to remote flight servers. It normalizes target URLs (default port 6464, path /ws, and automatic promotion to secure wss: on HTTPS pages), establishes reliable ordered text connections (TextConnection), validates incoming message lengths, and handles timeouts and cancellations via AbortSignal. The module is decoupled from flight mechanics, physics, UI rendering, and session semantics.

Provides and requires

  • Provides net.websocket@1.0.0 through the WEBSOCKET_TRANSPORT key: a TransportProvider instance for ws:// and wss:// schemes.
  • Contributes itself to the runtime transport registry via context.contributeTransport.
  • Requires no services.

Settings

  • timeoutMs (default 10000, from 1 to 60000, step 1): Maximum wait for a WebSocket connection to open in milliseconds. Does not alter the server heartbeat or session protocol.
  • maxTextLength (default 67108864, from 1 to 67108864, step 1): Maximum UTF-16 characters in a WebSocket text message. Lowering it can reject large snapshots or join packages; peers still enforce their own limits.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization provides one shared TransportProvider and registers it with the module context for owned disposal.
  • connect(address, signal) initiates an asynchronous handshake, returning a TextConnection on success.
  • TextConnection exposes label, bufferedAmount, send(text), close(reason), onmessage, and onclose.
  • Aborting the signal cancels pending connection attempts and closes any open socket.
  • Provider disposal cancels all in-flight connections and cleanly disconnects active peers with an AggregateError if cleanup fails.

Remove or replace

Removing this module disables WebSocket connectivity for multiplayer client sessions. Single-player games, offline scenarios, and local tab-to-tab multiplayer remain functional without it. To replace it, author a network-tier module that provides net.websocket@1.0.0 with a compatible TransportProvider implementation.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: network transport contracts can change before final release. Wire protocol compatibility is governed by the underlying WebSocket RFC 6455 standard.

Example

examples/modules/net-websocket demonstrates connecting to a WebSocket server with custom timeouts and validating incoming text messages. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/net-websocket/module.json /tmp/websocket-example.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/websocket-example.spacemod

Simulation-time orbital alarms §

Bounded isolated clocks with no engine or host commands.

What it does

This module provides simulation-time orbital reminder clocks and a detached read-only event catalogue. It tracks declared reminder deadlines and physical impulse event epochs against elapsed simulation seconds, delivering notices when deadlines pass without issuing engine commands, altering warp factors, or stepping the physics simulation.

Provides and requires

  • Provides flight.alarms@1.0.0 through the ORBITAL_ALARMS service key: creates independent OrbitalAlarmClock instances.
  • Provides flight.alarm-catalogue@1.0.0 through the ALARM_CATALOGUE service key: inspects registered alarms across sessions.
  • Fills the flight.alarms and flight.alarm-catalogue slots in presentation profiles.
  • Requires no external services in its module manifest.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization provides ORBITAL_ALARMS and ALARM_CATALOGUE implementations.
  • create() allocates an independent clock supporting up to 1024 concurrent events across up to 64 active clocks.
  • sync(session, now, events) synchronizes declared events against simulation time, returning due notices and tracking missed status.
  • ALARM_CATALOGUE.read(session) yields detached snapshots of active reminders for UI display.
  • dispose clears registered events and unbinds clock references.

Remove or replace

To customize alarm scheduling or reminder delivery policy, contribute alternative providers for flight.alarms@1.0.0 and flight.alarm-catalogue@1.0.0. Removing this module disables orbital event notifications while leaving maneuver planning functional.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: clock limit constants and event delivery structures may evolve prior to final API freeze.

Example

examples/modules/orbital-alarms demonstrates creating an orbital alarm clock, synchronizing events, and reading the catalogue. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/orbital-alarms/module.json /tmp/example-orbital-alarms.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-orbital-alarms.spacemod

Edit the orbital course §

Independent lesson content and simulation-checked objectives.

What it does

Publishes the 24-lesson Orbital Academy curriculum (spacelab.orbital-academy, version 1) across First flights, Orbital skills and Advanced tiers, together with the Players course guide. Lesson data is pure declarative content: each lesson defines an explicit practice placement (pad, circle, ellipse, approach or encounter on Primus or Parvus), optional target orbit ratios, physics notes and steps checked by scalar telemetry conditions or multiple-choice questions. The course never mutates world physics; unsupported custom worlds or non-analytic motion models receive clear diagnostics when starting a lesson. Automatic interplanetary transfers, 3D inclination changes, N-body/Lagrange dynamics and aerodynamic reentry are outside this 2D patched-conic course.

Provides and requires

  • Provides learning.orbital-course@1.0.0 through the ORBITAL_COURSE service key: exposes the immutable OrbitalCourse lesson catalogue.
  • Fills the learning.orbital-course slot in presentation and platform profiles.
  • Requires nothing. Consumers such as spacelab.academy-ui declare learning.orbital-course@^1.0.0; if no provider fills learning.orbital-course, dependent modules fail profile resolution.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization registers the static course object under ORBITAL_COURSE and contributes both the Players guide (docs/players/course.json) and this Creators guide (docs/creators/usage.json).
  • Consumers read course.id, course.version, course.title and course.lessons synchronously at mount time.
  • Increment an individual lesson's version when its completion criteria change so learners re-verify only that lesson, or increment course.version when progress storage must be separated across the entire course.
  • After editing src/index.ts, rebuild the package and run npm run academy:build to embed updated module archives into dist/orbital-academy.spacegame.

Remove or replace

Provide a replacement learning.orbital-course@1.0.0 module in the learning.orbital-course slot to supply custom lessons, practice orbits or knowledge checks without changing spacelab.academy-ui or spacelab.tutorial-runner. Removing this module is supported when no enabled module requires learning.orbital-course.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: lesson fields and observation telemetry keys may be extended before final freeze.

Example

examples/modules/orbital-lessons inspects the provided OrbitalCourse catalogue by difficulty tier and looks up lesson practice and target orbit metadata. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/orbital-lessons/module.json /tmp/example-orbital-lessons.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-orbital-lessons.spacemod

Rails and shared motion services §

Preserved numerical flight, analytic coasting, frame/contact services and independently replaceable rails policy.

What it does

This authoritative module connects the core simulation motion engine to standard module slots. It provides context creation for simulation stepping, Keplerian orbit and conic trajectory solvers, celestial body reference frame transformations, surface elevation and water contact detection, and conservative rails sleep scheduling for coasting craft.

Provides and requires

  • Provides builtin.motion@1.0.0 as the unified authoritative motion provider capability.
  • Provides motion@1.0.0 through the MOTION_CONTEXTS service key: creates per-ship simulation contexts.
  • Provides orbits@1.0.0 through the ORBITAL_MOTION service key: evaluates orbital elements and Keplerian propagation.
  • Provides bodies@1.0.0 through the BODY_FRAMES service key: resolves celestial body frames and SOI boundaries.
  • Provides surfaces@1.0.0 through the SURFACE_GEOMETRY service key: queries surface heights, bounds, and normal vectors.
  • Provides rails@1.0.0 through the RAILS_POLICY service key: computes wake horizons for inactive ships.
  • Fills the motion, orbits, bodies, surfaces, and rails slots in authoritative profiles.
  • Requires core.motion@^1.0.0: operates over the authoritative simulation kernel.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization queries context.use(CORE_MOTION) to access authoritative kernel services.
  • For each selected slot (motion, orbits, bodies, surfaces, rails), context.provide registers the corresponding service.
  • World simulation contexts invoke these services during physics ticks, trajectory predictions, and rails sleep evaluation.
  • Services remain stateless singletons bound for the lifetime of the running world session.

Remove or replace

Each motion slot can be replaced independently by custom modules providing motion@1.0.0, orbits@1.0.0, bodies@1.0.0, surfaces@1.0.0, or rails@1.0.0. Removing this module requires alternative providers for all motion capabilities required by the authoritative game profile.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: numerical tolerance limits and rails horizon wake heuristics may evolve prior to final API freeze.

Example

examples/modules/physics-rails demonstrates accessing motion services, creating a simulation context, and evaluating orbital elements. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/physics-rails/module.json /tmp/example-physics-rails.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-physics-rails.spacemod

Independent planet appearance designer §

Read-only body previews and viewer-scoped designs with explicit storage/export ports.

What it does

This module provides viewer-scoped planet appearance editing and 3D previewing over public read-only world contracts. It enables customizing celestial surface styles, color palettes, procedural seeds, and tuning parameters without modifying authoritative world state, simulation physics, or multiplayer network payloads. Custom designs and applied looks are saved to viewer-local storage under space-sim.planet-looks/1 and space-sim.planet-library/1, and can be exported as standalone space-sim-planets/1 JSON documents.

Provides and requires

  • Provides ui.planet-designer@1.0.0 through the PLANET_DESIGNERS service key: creates PlanetDesignerControl interactive designer instances.
  • Fills the ui.planet-designer slot in platform profiles.
  • Requires core.rotation@^1.0.0 through the CORE_ROTATION service key: evaluates celestial body spin rates and body-fixed orientations.
  • Requires render.webgl@^1.0.0 (optional) through the WEBGL_DRAWING service key: renders 3D sphere preview views.
  • Requires render.vector-shaders@^1.0.0 (optional) through the VECTOR_SHADERS service key: supplies shader program defaults for the preview.

Settings

  • previewSpinSeconds (default 20, from 1 to 120, step 1): Wall-clock seconds per decorative preview turn, in seconds, controlling rotational speed in the appearance preview without affecting physical world bodies.
  • sunDegrees (default 120, from 0 to 360, step 1): Initial lighting illumination angle, in degrees, setting the preview sun direction until user adjustment.
  • importMaxBytes (default 1048576, from 1024 to 8388608, step 1024): Maximum allowed file size when importing planet designs, in bytes, rejecting oversized files before parsing to protect memory.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization captures CORE_ROTATION, optional WEBGL_DRAWING, and optional VECTOR_SHADERS, loads stylesheet assets, registers PLANET_DESIGNERS, and contributes this guide.
  • create(options) mounts designer markup inside options.root, loads applied and library designs from viewer storage, and creates preview canvas elements.
  • appearanceFor(world, body) queries active custom looks for celestial bodies to override rendering appearance during flight or map viewing.
  • refresh() updates draft state against the current active body and re-renders preview canvases.
  • open() reveals the designer interface within the host layout.
  • dispose cancels preview animation frames, detaches event listeners, restores replaced DOM child nodes, and disposes preview renderers.

Remove or replace

To customize planet appearance editing or author custom celestial shaders, provide an alternative ui.planet-designer@1.0.0 implementation in the ui.planet-designer slot. Removing this module omits the planet appearance designer and preview editor; bodies display using default procedural appearance presets.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: preset definitions, export formats (space-sim-planets/1), and tuning ranges may evolve before final freeze.

Example

examples/modules/planet-designer demonstrates mounting the planet designer, querying appearance overrides, and handling lifecycle cleanup. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/planet-designer/module.json /tmp/example-planet-designer.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-planet-designer.spacemod

Player controller §

Routes namespaced intent to a compatible entity/session adapter without advancing a world.

What it does

This module routes player intent from borrowed input sources to a compatible control target adapter. It provides the input.controller service through the PLAYER_CONTROLLERS key. Adapters must conform to the spacelab-control-target/1 schema. It separates local device seats (pilots 0–3) from session player, seat, entity, and viewport identities. Action values are clamped to [-1, 1] and forwarded to adapter.setAction only when values change between frames. If session ownership is lost, the controller clears active intent and throws an ownership error. It does not advance simulation time or step physics worlds.

Provides and requires

  • Provides input.controller@1.0.0 through the PLAYER_CONTROLLERS key: factory creating player controllers over a spacelab-control-target/1 adapter.
  • The module itself requires no services.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization registers the PLAYER_CONTROLLERS service and sets up cleanup tracking for created controllers.
  • create validates adapter.schema === 'spacelab-control-target/1' and binds optional initial input sources.
  • select verifies session ownership via adapter.canControl and caches up to 256 namespaced action identifiers matching namespace.name/action. Switching target clears all held intent.
  • bindInput accepts at most 16 distinct sources for pilot 0–3, clearing prior device state.
  • sample runs synchronously across bound sources, enforcing a limit of 256 writes per source with last-write-wins semantics, and forwards changed values to adapter.setAction.
  • dispose releases target and device state. Provider disposal releases all created controllers, throwing an AggregateError if cleanup fails.

Remove or replace

Included in survey-fixture target plans. Games implementing custom input routing pipelines or networked client prediction can replace this module with another provider of input.controller@1.0.0.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: control target schema spacelab-control-target/1 and controller contracts may change before API freeze.

Example

examples/modules/player-controller is a creator example implementing a custom ControlTargetAdapter under schema spacelab-control-target/1 to command an entity through PLAYER_CONTROLLERS. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/player-controller/module.json /tmp/player-controller-example.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/player-controller-example.spacemod
