Customizable player views §

Independent input, camera, HUD and appearance adapters with shared identity and cleanup.

What it does

This module provides composite player view adapters unifying input controllers, camera tracking, HUD instruments, and vehicle appearance settings under a single identity lifecycle. It manages safe rebinding when players switch seats or targets, releasing gestures and held input intent before applying changes without creating or advancing simulation worlds.

Provides and requires

  • Provides ui.player-view@1.0.0 through the PLAYER_VIEWS service key: creates PlayerView instances and role adapters for controllers, cameras, and HUDs.
  • Fills the ui.player-view slot in platform configurations.
  • Requires no external services.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization contributes this guide and registers the PLAYER_VIEWS factory.
  • create(options) instantiates a composite PlayerView bound to a seat, viewport, and session identity.
  • controller, camera, and hud helper methods wrap underlying components into distinct role adapters with borrowed or owned lifecycles.
  • bind(binding) stops active gestures, releases old target intent, and safely rebinds each role adapter to the new target identity.
  • configure(adapters) replaces role adapters dynamically, reclaiming old resources and catching configuration faults.
  • dispose terminates the player view and safely disposes owned resources.

Remove or replace

To customize view lifecycle management, provide a module implementing ui.player-view@1.0.0 in the ui.player-view slot. Removing it requires hosts to manage per-player controller, camera, and HUD bindings individually.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: role adapter definitions and composite lifecycle hooks may undergo refinements prior to API freeze.

Example

examples/modules/player-view demonstrates wrapping camera, controller, and HUD adapters into a composite player view and rebinding target identities. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/player-view/module.json /tmp/example-player-view.spacemod

Frame profiler §

Optional bounded viewer diagnostics.

What it does

Provides optional viewer-local frame profilers for browser, offline and headless diagnostic consumers. A profiler times named frame sections with wall-clock milliseconds, keeps fixed ring buffers of 600 frames and 1000 events, and computes statistics only on request. Measurements never feed simulation or authoritative identity, and initialization needs no DOM, ship, world or renderer.

Provides and requires

  • Provides diagnostics.profiler@1.0.0 through the PROFILERS service key: creates independent frame profilers.
  • Fills the diagnostics.profiler slot in platform profiles.
  • Requires nothing. Consumers declare diagnostics.profiler@^1.0.0 and may omit it; removing the package leaves gameplay and renderer lifetime independent.

Settings

  • precise (default 0, from 0 to 1, step 1): 1 calls the optional flush callback at each lap so deferred raster work is attributed to the section that queued it. It is slower, and Chromium may keep the canvas unaccelerated until reload.
  • longWorkMs (default 11, from 1 to 1000, step 0.1): game work per frame above this many milliseconds flags a slow frame.
  • hitchMs (default 25, from 1 to 1000, step 0.1): a frame interval above this many milliseconds may be flagged as a hitch.
  • hitchRatio (default 2.5, from 1 to 10, step 0.1): a hitch must also exceed this multiple of the typical interval.
  • graphMs (default 33.4, from 8 to 1000, step 0.1): full height of drawGraph in milliseconds.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization reads settings, registers PROFILERS and contributes this guide.
  • create(flush, options) returns an independent profiler. Per frame, call begin(intervalMs) with the wall interval, lap(section) after each timed part and end() once.
  • summary(), group(sections), history() and log() compute or copy results on request; drawGraph(canvas) optionally renders recent frames. history() allocates; the frame path itself does not.
  • Disposing a profiler stops collection and releases retained events. Disposing the provider disposes every profiler and rejects new creation.

Remove or replace

Provide another diagnostics.profiler@1.0.0 implementation in the diagnostics.profiler slot to change measurement or reporting. Removing the module only removes profiling windows and exports; nothing else depends on it.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: details may change before the final freeze.

Example

examples/modules/profiler times frames and reads back a summary through the public diagnostics.profiler service. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/profiler/module.json /tmp/example-profiler.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-profiler.spacemod

Related

Code modules: manifests, packaging and installation (in-game Manual: modules)

API reference: Frame profiler

Canvas 2D §

Public render.canvas@1.0.0 service and lifecycle.

What it does

This module provides 2D scene and flight rendering using standard HTML5 Canvas 2D contexts. It projects double-precision celestial body positions, orbits, planetary surfaces, day and night lighting, craters, latitude parallels, trajectory lines, ship chevrons and throttle flames to screen coordinates using an independent camera transform. Render quality and pixel ratio caps never modify physical celestial bodies or collision geometry.

Provides and requires

  • Provides render.canvas@1.0.0 through the CANVAS_DRAWING key: a CanvasRendererFactory that creates SceneRenderer instances.
  • Requires core.motion@^1.0.0 through the CORE_MOTION key: binds surface terrain query ports for procedural elevation sampling.
  • Fills the render.canvas slot in presentation profiles.

Settings

  • maxPixelRatio (default 3, from 0.5 to 4, step 0.1): Maximum backing pixels per CSS pixel. Lower values reduce raster cost and memory; higher values sharpen high-DPI output. Collision geometry is unchanged.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization binds surface queries from CORE_MOTION, captures maxPixelRatio from settings, registers this guide, and provides CANVAS_DRAWING.
  • create(canvas, options) instantiates a CanvasRenderer wrapping the canvas 2D context. The factory registers each active renderer for cleanup.
  • drawScene(frame, timer) and draw(frame, timer) project celestial coordinates, draw procedural backdrops, bodies, orbit rings, overlays, ships and labels.
  • dispose marks the renderer disposed. Disposing the factory releases all tracked renderer instances.

Remove or replace

Canvas 2D is the default 2D rendering provider for targets without WebGL support. To replace it, implement a presentation module providing render.canvas@1.0.0 in the render.canvas slot. Alternatively, select render.webgl for GPU-accelerated rendering.

Stability

Module version 0.2.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: renderer interfaces and scene frame definitions can evolve before API freeze with documented migration paths.

Example

examples/modules/render-canvas demonstrates consuming render.canvas@1.0.0 to create a scene renderer, project celestial frames, and manage renderer disposal. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/render-canvas/module.json /tmp/example-canvas.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-canvas.spacemod

game-api rendering.ts: canvas renderer and scene frame contracts

API reference: Canvas 2D

WebGL 2: GPU-accelerated rendering and custom shaders

VehicleAppearance may declare metresPerUnit and local detail paths. Near-view hull/flame/details scale by the camera pixels per metre; sub-eight-pixel hulls become navigation markers. Match artwork to authoritative contactRadiusMetres; optional geometry never changes collision authority.

Tile chunks: features.tileChunks version 1 qualifies unlit 32x32 foreground tiles, optional rotationRadians about origin, and an optional simple clipPolygon in chunk-local metres before rotation (even-odd fill). Tiles, clip vertices and registered palettes are borrowed immutable data through drawing; dispose releases renderer resources. Check this feature before requesting clipping; absent support requires an explicit vector fallback or compatibility error. This subset does not qualify underground walls, light encoding or dense streaming. Canvas transforms full footprints; WebGL uses one instanced tile draw and one stencil mask draw per clipped chunk. Palette IDs used by the bounded successor profile are 0..4. Module layers validate finite rotation and detached clipping vertices; clip vertices share the existing 4,096-point layer budget with line vertices.

Tile conformance: actual features.tileChunks version 1 declares body-local rotation and simple even-odd clipPolygon. Optional lightEncoding=nibble, paletteEntries=1024, walls=true and buffers=borrowed-through-draw declare the supplied-light profile; requireTileChunkFeatures preflights essential consumers. Primitive lightEncoding=nibble validates 0..1023 IDs and 0..15 light, with brightness 0.2+0.8*n/15 and omitted light full. Omitted encoding retains legacy renderer lighting. Palette registration copies 1..1024 hex entries (#RGB/#RRGGBB/#RRGGBBAA); zero is transparent, missing slots use grey in strict mode and unknown palettes use the registered default. Cosmetic walls show only through air at 55% brightness; no underground light propagation or terrain authority is implied. Chunks draw before vector overlays and labels, outside the v1 shader-pack ABI. Arrays stay caller-owned and unchanged until all draws complete, then may be reused. Re-register palettes on renderer replacement; dispose releases resources. Missing optional tile support uses a declared vector fallback; no hardware-performance claim.

Optional RenderBody.air replaces legacy sky/glow, including mode off. Discover features.independentAir version 1 first. Modes air/clouds draw the same bounded radial shell in Canvas and WebGL, outside the union of canonical body ground masks and before trajectories/pads/hulls/overlay cues. Heights are metres above maximum terrain radius (or body radius); colors/opacity are normalized, phaseRadians is supplied in the body frame. The renderer never advances a clock or changes terrain/contact. Optional air.sunDirection uses the shared system direction without relighting ground. Missing air preserves legacy/Classic rendering. WebGL without stencil omits this optional capability; use an explicit clear/legacy fallback. No shader-pack ABI change, effect target, texture, physical weather or spherical projection is implied. The app controls low-tier cloud removal and pause/warp policy. See independentAirExample in examples/modules/render-canvas/src/main.ts. Near views include an opaque lighting-based sky (day/twilight/night), aligned to the local outward direction. It fades by altitude within heightMetres and by apparent body size for orbital maps. Canonical ground masking still applies; mode off retains the clear-space comparison.

Optional features.planetOverview version 1 accepts RenderBody.overview: coarse-to-fine body-fixed polar summaries, premultiplied linear RGBA, <=2048 entries per level and <=12 levels. Providers filter tiny bodies and shade an illustrative sphere inside unchanged canonical terrain/coasts; no new topology or collision. Canvas uses two radial gradient bands (<=1024 triangles); WebGL uses four (<=4096 triangles). Canonical ground remains complete when absent or unsupported, while air and gameplay cues retain their order. No fine chunks, new textures, effect targets or v1 shader ABI changes. Descriptor and arrays stay immutable for their lifetime; replace the descriptor object on palette/content revision. See planetOverviewExample in examples/modules/render-canvas/src/main.ts.

WebGL 2 §

Public render.webgl@1.0.0 service and lifecycle.

What it does

This module provides GPU-accelerated WebGL 2 rendering for flight scenes, celestial bodies and planet designer previews. It compiles multi-pass GLSL shader packs (scene, vector and text stages) and procedural planet materials. Shader u_time follows real presentation seconds even while physics is paused. Presentation options allow independently toggling orbit guides and body labels without altering celestial bodies or trajectory physics.

Provides and requires

  • Provides render.webgl@1.0.0 through the WEBGL_DRAWING key: a WebGLRendererFactory that creates SceneRenderer and BodyPreview instances.
  • Requires core.motion@^1.0.0 through the CORE_MOTION key: binds surface terrain query ports for procedural elevation sampling.
  • Fills the render.webgl slot in presentation profiles.

Settings

  • maxPixelRatio (default 2, from 0.5 to 4, step 0.1): Maximum backing pixels per CSS pixel for scene rendering. Lower values reduce GPU work and framebuffer memory. Does not change bodies or collision.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization binds surface queries from CORE_MOTION, captures maxPixelRatio from settings, registers this guide, and provides WEBGL_DRAWING.
  • create(canvas, shaders, options) compiles GLSL shader passes, initializes vertex buffers, noise textures and effect pipelines.
  • preview(canvas, shaders) compiles a background program for standalone planet designer rendering.
  • drawScene(frame, timer) and draw(frame, timer) execute GPU drawing passes across scene, vector and text stages.
  • dispose on a renderer or preview releases GL programs, buffers, textures and framebuffers. Disposing the factory releases all tracked instances.

Remove or replace

WebGL 2 is the primary rendering provider for hardware-accelerated games and the planet designer. To replace it, implement a presentation module providing render.webgl@1.0.0 in the render.webgl slot. Targets without WebGL 2 support fall back to render.canvas.

Stability

Module version 0.2.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: shader interfaces, effect limits and uniform conventions can evolve before API freeze with documented migration notes.

Example

examples/modules/render-webgl demonstrates consuming render.webgl@1.0.0 to initialize a WebGL 2 scene renderer with custom shader packs and manage renderer cleanup. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/render-webgl/module.json /tmp/example-webgl.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-webgl.spacemod

Canvas 2D: CPU fallback rendering

API reference: Vector shader pack

VehicleAppearance may declare metresPerUnit and local detail paths. Single and fleet vessels use physical world scale up close and navigation markers below eight projected CSS pixels. Match circular hull artwork with authoritative contactRadiusMetres; rendering does not define collision geometry.

Tile chunks: features.tileChunks version 1 qualifies unlit 32x32 foreground tiles, optional rotationRadians about origin, and an optional simple clipPolygon in chunk-local metres before rotation (even-odd fill). Tiles, clip vertices and registered palettes are borrowed immutable data through drawing; dispose releases renderer resources. Check this feature before requesting clipping; absent support requires an explicit vector fallback or compatibility error. This subset does not qualify underground walls, light encoding or dense streaming. Canvas transforms full footprints; WebGL uses one instanced tile draw and one stencil mask draw per clipped chunk. Palette IDs used by the bounded successor profile are 0..4. Module layers validate finite rotation and detached clipping vertices; clip vertices share the existing 4,096-point layer budget with line vertices.

Tile conformance: actual features.tileChunks version 1 declares body-local rotation and simple even-odd clipPolygon. Optional lightEncoding=nibble, paletteEntries=1024, walls=true and buffers=borrowed-through-draw declare the supplied-light profile; requireTileChunkFeatures preflights essential consumers. Primitive lightEncoding=nibble validates 0..1023 IDs and 0..15 light, with brightness 0.2+0.8*n/15 and omitted light full. Omitted encoding retains legacy renderer lighting. Palette registration copies 1..1024 hex entries (#RGB/#RRGGBB/#RRGGBBAA); zero is transparent, missing slots use grey in strict mode and unknown palettes use the registered default. Cosmetic walls show only through air at 55% brightness; no underground light propagation or terrain authority is implied. Chunks draw before vector overlays and labels, outside the v1 shader-pack ABI. Arrays stay caller-owned and unchanged until all draws complete, then may be reused. Re-register palettes on renderer replacement; dispose releases resources. Missing optional tile support uses a declared vector fallback; no hardware-performance claim.

Optional RenderBody.air replaces legacy sky/glow, including mode off. Discover features.independentAir version 1 first. Modes air/clouds draw the same bounded radial shell in Canvas and WebGL, outside the union of canonical body ground masks and before trajectories/pads/hulls/overlay cues. Heights are metres above maximum terrain radius (or body radius); colors/opacity are normalized, phaseRadians is supplied in the body frame. The renderer never advances a clock or changes terrain/contact. Optional air.sunDirection uses the shared system direction without relighting ground. Missing air preserves legacy/Classic rendering. WebGL without stencil omits this optional capability; use an explicit clear/legacy fallback. No shader-pack ABI change, effect target, texture, physical weather or spherical projection is implied. The app controls low-tier cloud removal and pause/warp policy. See independentAirExample in examples/modules/render-webgl/src/main.ts. Near views include an opaque lighting-based sky (day/twilight/night), aligned to the local outward direction. It fades by altitude within heightMetres and by apparent body size for orbital maps. Canonical ground masking still applies; mode off retains the clear-space comparison.

Optional features.planetOverview version 1 accepts RenderBody.overview: coarse-to-fine body-fixed polar summaries, premultiplied linear RGBA, <=2048 entries per level and <=12 levels. Providers filter tiny bodies and shade an illustrative sphere inside unchanged canonical terrain/coasts; no new topology or collision. Canvas uses two radial gradient bands (<=1024 triangles); WebGL uses four (<=4096 triangles). Canonical ground remains complete when absent or unsupported, while air and gameplay cues retain their order. No fine chunks, new textures, effect targets or v1 shader ABI changes. Descriptor and arrays stay immutable for their lifetime; replace the descriptor object on palette/content revision. See planetOverviewExample in examples/modules/render-webgl/src/main.ts.

Flight sessions §

Explicit flight protocol using privileged public engine ports and replaceable transports.

What it does

This module provides the core authoritative flight multiplayer protocol and session management. It manages local sessions, dedicated server hosts, and remote client sessions. A flight host borrows an existing simulation world, manages seated players, synchronizes ship state, dispatches flight commands, handles time warp acceleration, and broadcasts state snapshots over reliable text connections.

Provides and requires

  • Provides session.flight@1.0.0 through the FLIGHT_SESSIONS key: a FlightSessionFactory instance.
  • Requires core.world@^1.0.0 through CORE_WORLD: world lifecycle and entity management.
  • Requires core.save@^1.0.0 through CORE_SAVE: world state serialization.
  • Requires core.clock@^1.0.0 through CORE_CLOCK: simulation timing and scheduling.
  • Requires core.motion@^1.0.0 through CORE_MOTION: physical motion stepping.
  • Requires core.commands@^1.0.0 through CORE_COMMANDS: ship control command dispatch.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization binds the required engine ports and exposes the FlightSessionFactory.
  • host(world, settings, options) instantiates an authoritative FlightHost borrowing an existing WorldControl.
  • local(world, options) constructs a standalone single-player session owning its private world.
  • join(peer, options, signal) performs client-side handshake over a TextConnection, synchronizing entities into a local mirror world.
  • step() and advance(seconds) execute discrete ticks or wall-clock durations, returning world simulation events.
  • Disposing the factory or provider closes all active sessions and disconnects peers.

Remove or replace

This module is essential for flight multiplayer sessions and standard flight gameplay. Standalone non-flight tools like planet viewers do not require it. To replace it, author a network-tier module providing session.flight@1.0.0 implementing FlightSessionFactory.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze. Wire communication uses protocol identifier space-sim-net/6 and entity schema space-sim-flight-entities/1.

Example

examples/modules/session-flight demonstrates creating an authoritative flight host over a simulation world and asserting pause and step controls. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/session-flight/module.json /tmp/session-flight-example.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/session-flight-example.spacemod

Install on join §

Bounded, versioned negotiation before session construction and exact package approval.

What it does

This module coordinates pre-join game pack distribution between multiplayer hosts and connecting clients before the flight session starts. It transmits structured package offers listing required modules, licenses, and digests. When the client requires missing assets, it transfers chunked archive data, validates SHA-256 digests, requests user approval via review hooks, and stages modules in isolation without affecting global configuration.

Provides and requires

  • Provides session.join-pack@1.0.0 through the JOIN_PACKS key: a JoinPackFactory instance.
  • Requires no services.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization provides the JoinPackFactory and registers it for owned disposal.
  • client(peer, port, signal) reads the initial wire message; legacy servers sending 'info' pass through immediately, while prejoin servers negotiate offers.
  • The client validates code permissions against port.allowRuntimeCode, checks local caches, and prompts the user via port.review.
  • port.stage prepares the verified modules in a temporary staging container (JoinStage).
  • Upon successful authentication, calling stage.activate() publishes the newly acquired modules into active runtime use.
  • host(peer, source, accept, signal) serves package offers and chunks, completing with accept(peer) once the client signals readiness.
  • Provider disposal aborts all pending negotiations with an error.

Remove or replace

Removing this module disables automatic server package downloads when joining custom multiplayer worlds. Connecting to vanilla servers without custom packs remains operational. To replace it, author a network-tier module providing session.join-pack@1.0.0 implementing JoinPackFactory.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze. Negotiation follows protocol spacelab-prejoin/1 and offer format spacelab-join-offer/1.

Example

examples/modules/session-join demonstrates executing client-side prejoin negotiation with mock package verification. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/session-join/module.json /tmp/session-join-example.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/session-join-example.spacemod

Vector shaders §

Public render.vector-shaders@1.0.0 service and lifecycle.

What it does

This module provides the baseline GLSL shader assets for the three-pass rendering pipeline: scene (celestial bodies, terrain masks and lighting), vector (orbits, prediction traces, heading chevrons and UI lines), and text (glyph quads). It exposes an immutable default ShaderPack along with validation and parsing utilities for format space-sim-shaders/1.

Provides and requires

  • Provides render.vector-shaders@1.0.0 through the VECTOR_SHADERS key: a ShaderLibrary offering defaults, validate(value), and parse(text, filename).
  • The module requires no services and loads no external dependencies.
  • Fills the render.vector-shaders slot in presentation profiles.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization reads the six bundled GLSL stage assets from module storage, bundles them into an immutable default ShaderPack, validates the pack, registers this guide, and provides VECTOR_SHADERS.
  • No graphics context or WebGL context is initialized; GPU compilation is deferred to renderer creation.
  • validate and parse return frozen, verified ShaderPack records without allocating persistent caches.
  • The library is stateless and owns no disposable handles.

Remove or replace

This module is not loaded by default in standard gameplay targets, but serves as the shader pack provider for WebGL rendering and custom visual mods. To replace default shaders, implement a presentation module providing render.vector-shaders@1.0.0 or pass custom ShaderPack objects to WebGLRendererFactory.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze. Uses shader pack format space-sim-shaders/1.

Example

examples/modules/shaders-vector demonstrates reading default shader stages, parsing custom GLSL fragment replacements, and validating partial shader packs. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/shaders-vector/module.json /tmp/example-shaders.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-shaders.spacemod

Body spin presentation §

Optional anti-strobe display over pure prescribed sidereal rotation.

What it does

This module provides presentation smoothing for rapidly rotating celestial bodies during high time warp. When a body's apparent rotation rate exceeds a visual threshold, it holds the drawn angle visually still to prevent stroboscopic optical illusions and jarring camera spins while preserving exact physical rotation in Keplerian simulation.

Provides and requires

  • Provides view.spin@1.0.0 through the SPIN_VIEWS service key: creates SpinViewControl smoothing instances.
  • Requires core.rotation@^1.0.0 through the CORE_ROTATION service key: uses rotation mathematics for spin rates and body-fixed transformations.
  • Fills the view.spin slot in presentation profiles.

Settings

  • holdRadiansPerFrame (default 0.08726646259971647, from 0.01 to 0.5): Hold a rapidly spinning body visually still above this turn per displayed frame, avoiding strobing during warp. Physics always uses its true angle.
  • resumeRatio (default 0.6, from 0.1 to 0.9, step 0.05): Resume visible rotation below this fraction of the hold threshold, reducing flicker around the transition.
  • releaseSeconds (default 0.4, from 0 to 2, step 0.05): Wall seconds to ease a held body back to its true angle. Zero snaps immediately; physical rotation and saves are unchanged.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization captures holdRadiansPerFrame, resumeRatio, and releaseSeconds settings, binds CORE_ROTATION, contributes this guide, and registers SPIN_VIEWS.
  • create() allocates independent SpinViewControl controllers.
  • update(bodies, time, elapsed, instant) samples spin rates, detects high-warp rotation thresholds, and eases offsets during release.
  • angle, point, and state transform body-fixed positions and resting ships into visually stabilized presentation coordinates.
  • dispose resets angle offsets, release easings, and tracked body state.

Remove or replace

To replace spin presentation behavior, provide view.spin@1.0.0 in the view.spin slot. Removing this module causes celestial bodies to be drawn at their exact unbuffered physical angle at all warp speeds, which may induce visual strobing during fast forward.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: easing functions and angle offset interpolation hooks may evolve before API freeze.

Example

examples/modules/spin-view demonstrates instantiating a spin-view controller, stepping warp presentation, and querying stabilized body angles. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/spin-view/module.json /tmp/example-spin-view.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-spin-view.spacemod

Related

Code modules: manifests, packaging and installation (in-game Manual: modules)

Themes §

Public presentation.themes@1.0.0 service and lifecycle.

What it does

This module provides presentation theme management, CSS custom properties injection, and procedural starry backdrop rendering. It controls UI styling for window frames, telemetry counters, HUD phosphor glows, CRT scanlines, and coordinate grids. It renders multi-layer starfields and animated nebulae into canvas contexts without modifying celestial physics, body geometry or game state.

Provides and requires

  • Provides presentation.themes@1.0.0 through the THEMES key: a ThemeFactory that creates isolated ThemeController instances.
  • The module requires no services and loads no external dependencies.
  • Fills the presentation.themes slot in presentation profiles.

Settings

  • windowOpacity (default 0.96, from 0.4 to 1, step 0.01): Opacity for viewers without a saved window preference. Saved viewer choices take precedence. Applies to presentation only.
  • starDensity (default 1, from 0.5 to 2, step 0.05): Background star-density multiplier for viewers without a saved choice. These decorative stars have no physical bodies or collision.
  • nebulaIntensity (default 1, from 0 to 2, step 0.05): Brightness of the procedural nebula background before viewer preferences override it. Does not affect lighting physics.
  • gridOpacity (default 0.45, from 0 to 1, step 0.01): Opacity of the optional background grid before viewer preferences override it. This does not turn the grid on.
  • gridSpacing (default 80, from 8 to 512, step 1): Screen-space grid spacing in CSS pixels before viewer preferences override it. Does not change world coordinates or scale.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization bundles theme.css and hud.css stylesheet assets, initializes the ThemeFactory with module settings, registers this guide, and provides THEMES.
  • create(options) instantiates an isolated ThemeController, leasing an injected style tag in the host document head.
  • load() retrieves saved preferences from storage, applies CSS custom properties to the DOM root, and returns active settings.
  • set(value) updates theme options, persists preferences to storage, updates CSS variables, and notifies change listeners.
  • backdrop(...) paints procedural stars and nebulae into canvas contexts.
  • dispose on a controller unbinds listeners and releases its style tag lease. Disposing the factory cleans up all controllers.

Remove or replace

Presentation theme styling is optional. Headless servers and testing profiles omit presentation modules entirely. To replace the theme engine, provide presentation.themes@1.0.0 with a custom ThemeFactory implementation.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze. Uses storage key space-sim:theme:v5 for backward compatibility with existing player preferences.

Example

examples/modules/theme demonstrates creating an isolated ThemeController, loading curated presets, mutating theme settings, and subscribing to change events. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/theme/module.json /tmp/example-theme.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-theme.spacemod

Theme controls §

Public ui.theme-customizer@1.0.0 service and lifecycle.

What it does

This module provides a modal settings dialog for visual theme customization. It allows players and creators to switch between built-in presets (Spacelab, Cyber, Apollo, Tactical, Aurora, Solar, Void), tune backdrop parameters (star density, nebula intensity, grid spacing, window opacity), toggle scanlines, and import or export custom theme configurations.

Provides and requires

  • Provides ui.theme-customizer@1.0.0 through the THEME_CUSTOMIZERS key: a ThemeCustomizerFactory that creates ThemeCustomizer dialog instances.
  • The module requires no services at initialization. Each created dialog requires a ThemeController and a host DOM element.
  • Fills the ui.theme-customizer slot in platform profiles.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization bundles the controls.css stylesheet asset, registers this guide, and provides THEME_CUSTOMIZERS.
  • create(options) mounts dialog controls into options.root and registers event listeners for presets, sliders and import/export buttons.
  • open(), close(), and toggle() control dialog visibility.
  • toggleGrid() toggles celestial grid visibility on the bound theme controller.
  • dispose removes dialog elements from the host DOM, unbinds event listeners and cancels active timers.

Remove or replace

Theme controls are optional platform tools. Games that provide custom styling dialogs or lock visuals can replace or omit ui.theme-customizer without affecting physics, flight controls or world state.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: dialog markup, events and options may evolve before API freeze with documented migration notes.

Example

Throttle HUD §

A per-seat throttle lever that any game can place in its HUD through the hud.throttle service.

What it does

This module draws the vertical THR lever beside each local player's view. A player drags it, or focuses it and uses the arrow, Page and Home/End keys, to set engine thrust from 0 to 100%. It is an instrument: a small piece of HUD that a HUD owner creates once per seat (one local player) and viewport, then updates every rendered frame. The module reads flight data and sends throttle requests only through an adapter that the game supplies, so it never touches the world, physics or saves.

Provides and requires

  • Provides hud.throttle@1.0.0 through the THROTTLE_HUD key: a HudInstrumentDefinition with ID spacelab.throttle-hud.
  • The module itself requires no services. Each lever it creates needs the view.flight-hud-state@1.0.0 adapter (FLIGHT_HUD_STATE), which the game passes to the HUD owner for every seat. A HUD without that adapter skips the lever instead of failing.
  • The HUD owner is ui.view-hud@1.0.0 (VIEW_HUD, from spacelab.view-hud). Space Sim and Flight starter select THROTTLE_HUD into every seat's view HUD.

Settings

  • keyboardStepPercent (default 1, from 0.1 to 100, step 0.1): percentage points added or removed by one Up or Down arrow press on a focused lever.
  • pageStepPercent (default 10, from 0.1 to 100, step 0.1): percentage points added or removed by one Page Up or Page Down press. Home always selects 0% and End 100%.
  • Both settings only change keyboard steps. Session ownership and input locks still decide whether a request is applied.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization provides one shared, stateless instrument definition and this guide. The module owns no resources of its own.
  • create runs once per seat. It builds the lever element and its listeners; the first update attaches it to the HUD surface root.
  • rebind (a new target, player or session) and cancel stop any drag in progress. A drag also stops when the target ship changes or the adapter reports the lever as locked.
  • update runs every frame with wall-clock seconds. It reads the adapter's throttle state and positions the lever; the game may move it with placeThrottle, for example to follow a saved HUD layout. draw adds no overlay shapes.
  • dispose removes the element and its window listener. The HUD owner disposes a lever when its seat is removed, when the instrument is deselected, or when one of its methods throws.

Remove or replace

Space Sim and Flight starter list hud.throttle as a required slot, so their targets need a provider. Players hide the levers with Settings → HUD visibility → Throttle levers, which keeps the module loaded. Physics, saves, the navball, cameras and split screen do not depend on the lever.

To replace it, write a presentation module that lists hud.throttle in slots and provides hud.throttle@1.0.0 with its own HudInstrumentDefinition, then select it in the game profile with "providers": { "hud.throttle": "your.module-id" }. Send thrust only through FlightHudAdapter.setThrottle so that ownership checks still apply.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: the HUD contracts can still change before the API is frozen, and a breaking change will come with a migration note. The legacy presentation ID builtin.throttle is a compatibility record that keeps the on/off choice in older presentation profiles; new code uses THROTTLE_HUD.

Example

examples/modules/throttle-hud is a minimal replacement provider: a text readout with − and + buttons that fills hud.throttle@1.0.0. It shows the full instrument lifecycle and reads its step from a setting. Build and check it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/throttle-hud/module.json /tmp/throttle-readout.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/throttle-readout.spacemod

Related

Code modules: manifests, packaging and installation (in-game Manual: modules)

Bounded orbital transfer search §

Editable bounded same-body and interbody transfers over selected moving-frame prediction contracts.

What it does

This module provides a generator-based bounded solver evaluating circular, elliptical, same-body, and interbody transfer trajectories and intermediate parking orbits over verified prediction contracts. It compares departure apsides, solves tangential velocity increments, checks terrain contact clearances, and structures candidate routes into efficient and faster options.

Provides and requires

  • Provides flight.transfers@1.0.0 through the TRANSFERS service key: creates TransferPlanner generator instances.
  • Fills the flight.transfers slot in presentation profiles.
  • Requires flight.plans@^1.0.0: integrates with flight plan node creation and route evaluation.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization resolves the flight plan service and provides TRANSFERS.
  • withBurns(factory) optionally binds a finite maneuver burn factory to refine burn durations and capture validation.
  • search(world, request) returns a generator yielding intermediate TransferProgress objects and returning a final TransferResult.
  • Each generator step performs bounded cooperative search calculations without advancing live simulation time.
  • Search cancellation terminates child contexts and releases allocated prediction seeds.

Remove or replace

To customize orbital transfer trajectory search algorithms or add specialized planetary encounter heuristics, contribute an alternative provider for flight.transfers@1.0.0 to the flight.transfers slot. Removing this module disables transfer window searches while preserving manual node editing.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: search iteration limits and route option data structures may evolve prior to final API freeze.

Example

examples/modules/transfer-planner demonstrates querying transfer solver capabilities and stepping through transfer search progress. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/transfer-planner/module.json /tmp/example-transfer-planner.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-transfer-planner.spacemod

Reusable tutorial runner §

Read-only objectives, versioned local progress and explicit knowledge checks.

What it does

Provides a bounded, read-only tutorial state machine that evaluates scalar flight observations and multiple-choice knowledge checks without touching simulation state, DOM elements, timers or session storage directly. Each lesson step requires either simulation-checked conditions or an explicit knowledge question. Timed holds accrue only across consecutive unpaused simulation seconds, and any change in flight continuity identity or simulation time rewind interrupts the active attempt.

Provides and requires

  • Provides learning.runner@1.0.0 through the TUTORIAL_RUNNERS service key: creates independent TutorialRunner instances.
  • Fills the learning.runner slot in presentation and platform profiles.
  • Requires nothing. Consumers such as spacelab.academy-ui declare learning.runner@^1.0.0; if no provider fills learning.runner, dependent modules fail profile resolution.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization registers TUTORIAL_RUNNERS and contributes this guide.
  • create({ storage, storageKey }) loads up to 256 completed lesson strings (each at most 160 characters) from storageKey; unavailable or corrupt storage falls back silently to an in-memory Set.
  • start(lesson, observation) validates that lesson has 1 to 64 steps, each step has either 1 to 32 conditions or a valid question (never both), holdSeconds is finite and non-negative, and allowPaused steps have holdSeconds === 0; it then records observation.values as the baseline and observation.identity as the continuity token.
  • update(observation) interrupts the attempt if observation.identity changed, observation.time is non-finite or observation.time decreased; otherwise it checks every condition against current and baseline values and advances once held for holdSeconds consecutive unpaused simulation seconds (or immediately when allowPaused is true).
  • answer(choice) checks the 0-based choice index against the current step's question, sets explanation feedback and advances on a correct answer; when the final step completes, <lesson.id>@<lesson.version> is added to completed and persisted.
  • stop() clears the active lesson and hold state; dispose() stops the runner and causes subsequent start() calls to throw.

Remove or replace

Provide another learning.runner@1.0.0 implementation in the learning.runner slot to customize condition evaluation or progress persistence. Removing this module is supported when no enabled module requires learning.runner; if spacelab.academy-ui is enabled, removing learning.runner prevents profile resolution.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: condition operators and runner diagnostics may be refined before final freeze. Completion entries use the versioned <lesson.id>@<lesson.version> format.

Example

examples/modules/tutorial-runner creates a runner, evaluates a timed scalar hold objective and answers a knowledge check through learning.runner. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/tutorial-runner/module.json /tmp/example-tutorial-runner.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-tutorial-runner.spacemod

Related

Code modules: manifests, packaging and installation (in-game Manual: modules)

API reference: Tutorial runner

game-api: tutorials.ts

Window shell and dock §

Optional grouped dock and window lifecycle.

What it does

Provides a window shell: grouped panels, a dock of buttons, optional instrument windows, modal Escape handling, fullscreen-Escape handling and import-to-panel routing, all on top of shared windows. It creates no game-specific panels; the host supplies panel definitions, borrowed content nodes and callbacks. The game can omit the shell entirely, and nothing here touches gameplay or session authority.

Provides and requires

  • Provides ui.shell@1.0.0 through the WINDOW_SHELL service key: creates window shells for a viewport.
  • Fills the ui.shell slot in platform profiles.
  • Requires ui.windows@^1.0.0 through the WINDOWS service key: supplies window chrome and icons.
  • Also exports createActionDock for grouped game-specific actions that share native vector button chrome.

Settings

  • escapeToggleMs (default 300, from 0 to 1000, step 1): minimum wall-time interval in milliseconds between Escape or fullscreen toggles. Raising it ignores rapid repeats; 0 allows every press.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization captures WINDOWS, registers WINDOW_SHELL and contributes this guide.
  • create(options) builds one shell for a viewport from grouped panel specs and borrowed content nodes. The shell owns its windows, dock entries, subscriptions, keyboard and file listeners and styles.
  • addInstrument(window, label, hint) adds an optional dock entry for a borrowed window and returns a lease; dispose the lease when the instrument is removed. The shell does not own borrowed instrument windows.
  • open, close, toggle and handleFullscreenEscape drive the panels; release and focus are host callbacks for input.
  • Disposing a shell, or the provider, removes listeners and styles and restores borrowed nodes to their original locations. A dock lease restores the controls it borrowed.

Remove or replace

Provide another ui.shell@1.0.0 implementation in the ui.shell slot to change how panels and the dock look and behave. Removing the module leaves the game without a grouped dock; any host code that requires ui.shell must handle that, while gameplay and sessions are unaffected.

Stability

Module version 0.2.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: details may change before the final freeze.

Example

examples/modules/ui-shell wraps one panel in a shell through the public ui.shell service, showing the factory contract and borrowed-content lifecycle. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/ui-shell/module.json /tmp/example-ui-shell.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-ui-shell.spacemod

game-api: windows.ts

Shared windows §

Independent movable, resizable and disposable windows through public ui.windows@1.

What it does

Provides shared floating windows and structured window content. Windows give independent chrome (moving, resizing, pin, minimize, maximize, stacking) around host-supplied containers. Window content mounts documents, instructions, inspectors, forms, lists, logs and versioned previews as plain text and DOM. The module owns layout chrome only; layout preferences never carry authority, and titles and all content strings are rendered as text.

Provides and requires

  • Provides ui.windows@1.0.0 through the WINDOWS service key: creates windows and one shared window system per factory.
  • Provides ui.window-content@1.0.0 through the WINDOW_CONTENT service key: mounts structured content and registers preview types.
  • Fills the ui.windows and ui.window-content slots in platform profiles.
  • Requires nothing.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization registers each service only when selected for its slot and contributes this guide.
  • WINDOWS.create(options) returns one window for a container, ID and title, with optional viewer preference storage. Windows created by one system share a stack; each provider has an independent stack, so borrowed chat window interactions use the same WindowSystem.
  • WINDOW_CONTENT.mount and document mount content into a root and return a lease. register({type:{name,version},validate,mount}) publishes an exact-version preview type; its mounts own their camera and renderer, receive immutable bounded data and must dispose every read-only subscription. Missing versions fail preparation; unregistering closes all instances of that type.
  • Dispose window and content leases on removal. Disposing the provider closes remaining windows, releases styles and stops retained callbacks. A game supplies its own dismiss gesture, theme decoration and dock.

Remove or replace

Provide another ui.windows@1.0.0 or ui.window-content@1.0.0 implementation in the matching slot to restyle or replace chrome or content rendering. Removing this module removes shared windows from every module that requires them, including the shell and the Manual.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: details may change before the final freeze.

Example

examples/modules/ui-windows opens a movable notice window through the public ui.windows service and disposes it. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/ui-windows/module.json /tmp/example-ui-windows.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-ui-windows.spacemod

Universe file storage §

Bounded browser files and owned downloads, independent of save validation.

What it does

This module provides bounded browser file reading and download triggering for universe save archives. It reads user-selected Blobs up to 64 MiB into byte arrays and dispatches downloads by creating temporary object URLs for generated archives. It tracks object URLs and revokes them upon a 10-second timeout or immediate provider disposal. It performs no decoding, world inspection, package validation, or engine state activation.

Provides and requires

  • Provides universe.files@1.0.0 through the UNIVERSE_FILES service key: creates UniverseFiles file reading and download services.
  • Fills the universe.files slot in platform profiles.
  • Requires no additional services.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization registers the UNIVERSE_FILES service and contributes this guide.
  • read(file, signal) validates that the Blob size does not exceed 64 MiB, checks for cancellation, and resolves array buffer bytes.
  • write(name, bytes, signal) validates filename length and characters, creates an object URL, clicks a temporary anchor tag to start the download, and schedules automatic URL revocation after 10 seconds.
  • dispose clears pending revocation timers, revokes all tracked object URLs, and causes subsequent operations to throw.

Remove or replace

To customize file transfer or route archives to cloud or IndexedDB storage, contribute an alternative universe.files@1.0.0 provider to the universe.files slot. Removing this module leaves browser save interfaces without file input reading or download triggering.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: object URL timeouts and filename validation patterns may evolve before final freeze.

Example

examples/modules/universe-files demonstrates reading and writing universe archive bytes through the UNIVERSE_FILES provider. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/universe-files/module.json /tmp/example-universe-files.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-universe-files.spacemod

game-api: universe-saving.ts

Universe save controls §

Independent Save universe / Load universe UI over explicit commands and file storage.

What it does

This module provides mounting for Save universe and Load universe buttons and a file selection picker into a DOM container element. It connects user click and file selection events to caller-owned universe save commands and browser file reader and download services. It tracks busy state to disallow concurrent operations, invokes caller callbacks on success or failure, and cleanly restores original DOM children upon disposal.

Provides and requires

  • Provides ui.universe-saves@1.0.0 through the UNIVERSE_SAVE_CONTROLS service key: mounts Save and Load universe UI controls.
  • Fills the ui.universe-saves slot in platform profiles.
  • Requires universe.files@^1.0.0 through the UNIVERSE_FILES service key: performs browser file reading and download triggering.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization captures UNIVERSE_FILES, registers UNIVERSE_SAVE_CONTROLS, and contributes this guide.
  • mount(options) borrows caller-provided buttons or creates default Save universe and Load universe DOM buttons and a hidden file input inside options.root.
  • Save button click triggers commands.save using files.write, notifying options.saved upon successful completion.
  • Load button click triggers file input picker, and file selection invokes commands.load using files.read, notifying options.loaded.
  • dispose detaches event listeners, aborts in-flight control operations, restores original DOM children, and clears control references.

Remove or replace

To customize save dialog styling or replace default buttons with a custom menu widget, provide an alternative ui.universe-saves@1.0.0 implementation in the ui.universe-saves slot. Removing this module omits the default Save and Load DOM controls from pause menus and navigation shells.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: button identifiers, prefixes, and event handling semantics may evolve before final freeze.

Example

examples/modules/universe-save-controls demonstrates mounting Save and Load controls and handling operation callbacks. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/universe-save-controls/module.json /tmp/example-universe-save-controls.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-universe-save-controls.spacemod

game-api: universe-saving.ts

Universe save commands §

Cancellable save/load over engine validation and independently selected storage.

What it does

This module provides single-operation save and load command controllers for universe sessions. It coordinates world state serialization and container encoding while restricting each controller to at most one in-flight operation. It verifies session and world identity guards prior to publication, ensuring that session transitions occurring during asynchronous operations abort safely without corrupting active worlds. The host retains package recovery, container decoding safety, and engine activation.

Provides and requires

  • Provides universe.saves@1.0.0 through the UNIVERSE_SAVES service key: creates UniverseSaveCommands controllers.
  • Fills the universe.saves slot in platform profiles.
  • Requires core.save@^1.0.0 through the CORE_SAVE service key: serializes active simulation state into WorldSave records.
  • Requires core.universe-save-format@^1.0.0 through the CORE_UNIVERSE_FORMAT service key: encodes and decodes container archive bytes.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization captures CORE_SAVE and CORE_UNIVERSE_FORMAT, registers UNIVERSE_SAVES, and contributes this guide.
  • create(options) instantiates a UniverseSaveCommands controller tracking single-operation execution and active session identity.
  • save(write, signal) captures session snapshot identity, invokes core.save, encodes container bytes, verifies identity guard, and writes formatted .spaceuniverse files.
  • load(read, signal) invokes read, enforces the 64 MiB limit, decodes container data via format.decode, checks identity guard, and restores the world.
  • dispose cancels pending save or load tasks and disassociates active controllers.

Remove or replace

To customize save orchestration, permissions checking, or session migration rules, provide an alternative universe.saves@1.0.0 implementation in the universe.saves slot. Removing this module leaves universe save and load workflows without coordinated session guards and format delegation.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: guard checks and error reporting may be adjusted before final freeze.

Example

examples/modules/universe-saves demonstrates creating save commands, executing serialized saves, and verifying session guards. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/universe-saves/module.json /tmp/example-universe-saves.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-universe-saves.spacemod

Server universe storage §

Serialized atomic saves, backups and validated recovery over borrowed native disk IO.

What it does

This module provides persistent server-side universe storage for Node environments. It manages serialized asynchronous disk writes via temporary file writes and atomic replacement, maintains timestamped historical backups, quarantines corrupted save files, and attempts automatic recovery from prior backups. It distinguishes engine and package configuration errors from save corruption, ensuring missing packages do not result in quarantined data.

Provides and requires

  • Provides universe.server-storage@1.0.0 through the SERVER_UNIVERSE_STORAGE service key: creates ServerUniverseStore storage instances.
  • Fills the universe.server-storage slot in platform profiles.
  • Requires core.universe-disk@^1.0.0 through the CORE_UNIVERSE_DISK service key: performs low-level native filesystem IO operations.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization captures CORE_UNIVERSE_DISK, registers SERVER_UNIVERSE_STORAGE, and contributes this guide.
  • create(options) instantiates a ServerUniverseStore instance bound to options.directory and backup retention settings.
  • save(save) enqueues serialized world data writes, writing to a temporary file and atomically renaming it to world.json.
  • load() reads world.json, invokes options.restore, falls back to timestamped backups if corrupt, and rethrows configuration errors without quarantine.
  • backup() copies the current active world file into a timestamped backup and trims historical backups beyond keepBackups.
  • idle() returns a Promise resolving when all currently queued disk writes have completed.
  • dispose marks the store closed, rejecting subsequent writes and unregistering active references.

Remove or replace

To customize server universe persistence (such as backing by a relational database or external object store), provide an alternative universe.server-storage@1.0.0 implementation in the universe.server-storage slot. Removing this module omits automated disk persistence and backup rotation in dedicated Node servers.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: backup file naming formats and quarantine conventions may be refined before final freeze.

Example

examples/modules/universe-server-storage demonstrates creating a server universe store, saving world state, and managing backups. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/universe-server-storage/module.json /tmp/example-universe-server-storage.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-universe-server-storage.spacemod

Portable trainer §

Per-craft handling over shared physics, with explicit save/session identity.

What it does

This authoritative module contributes the Orbit Trainer vehicle definition and accompanying flight guides. It registers spacelab.trainer/orbit-trainer into the world's vehicle catalog, inheriting system vehicle defaults for mass, thrust acceleration, and turn rate without modifying simulation mechanics.

Provides and requires

  • Provides no module capabilities directly; contributes the Orbit Trainer vehicle definition via contributeVehicle.
  • Declares no module slots.
  • Requires core.motion@^1.0.0: operates within simulation motion contexts.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization calls context.contributeVehicle(trainer) to register the Orbit Trainer vehicle definition.
  • Registers player and creator usage guides with the manual.
  • World creation incorporates the registered vehicle definition into the authoritative vehicle catalog.
  • The vehicle definition remains active for the duration of the simulation session.

Remove or replace

To add or customize vehicle handling, contribute alternative vehicle definitions using context.contributeVehicle. Removing this module omits the default orbit trainer craft; another module must provide at least one valid vehicle definition.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: vehicle definition fields and catalog registry contracts may evolve prior to final API freeze.

Example

examples/modules/vehicle-trainer demonstrates registering a vehicle definition and querying the vehicle catalog. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/vehicle-trainer/module.json /tmp/example-vehicle-trainer.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-vehicle-trainer.spacemod

API reference: Orbit trainer

game-api: vehicles.ts

game-api: engine.ts

VehicleDefaults and per-vehicle handling accept optional contactRadiusMetres (0–32 m). Omitted/zero preserves point contact; finite hulls use a circular footprint with centre-based state, bounded continuous dry-terrain contact, radius-aware spawn/support/prediction/analytic guards and a distinct motion identity. Ocean combinations fail explicitly. Footprint authority must agree with metre-based artwork; legacy saves/settings remain unchanged.

Shared view HUD lifecycle §

Optional instruments with independent per-view lifetimes.

What it does

This module provides a robust multi-instrument HUD manager with independent lifetimes per seat and viewport. It coordinates resizing, input gesture cancellations, updates, and overlay drawing across contributed flight instruments while isolating instrument faults so an error in one instrument never disrupts others or crashes the host view.

Provides and requires

  • Provides ui.view-hud@1.0.0 through the VIEW_HUD service key: creates ViewHud managers for viewport HUD surfaces.
  • Fills the ui.view-hud slot in platform configurations.
  • Requires no external services.

Settings

This module has no settings.

Lifecycle

  • Initialization contributes this guide and registers the VIEW_HUD factory.
  • create(options) instantiates a ViewHud controller bound to a viewport and seat identity.
  • select(definitions) dynamically activates declared instruments matching available service adapters and tears down unselected instruments.
  • resize(surface) adjusts CSS-pixel dimensions and dispatches new surface bounds to active instruments.
  • update(elapsed) steps instrument logic with wall-clock elapsed seconds.
  • draw(into) collects vector overlay primitives; shapes appended before a fault are discarded.
  • dispose terminates all active instruments cleanly.

Remove or replace

To customize HUD instrument hosting, implement ui.view-hud@1.0.0 in the ui.view-hud slot. Removing this module disables instrument aggregation and overlays across flight viewports.

Stability

Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: instrument definition schemas and fault notification interfaces may evolve before API freeze.

Example

examples/modules/view-hud demonstrates creating a ViewHud instance, selecting instrument definitions, and drawing overlays. Build and validate it from the repository root:

npm run module:build -- examples/modules/view-hud/module.json /tmp/example-view-hud.spacemod
npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-view-hud.spacemod
